<meta>This page continues the catalog of physical states, moving from the rest and wakefulness discussed on the previous page to the conditions of the body regarding health, appetite, and illness. It follows a logical progression from the sensation of hunger to the state of being satiated, and finally into the realm of sickness and physical pain.</meta>

**Sickly** <note>original: *Morbidus*</note>, one who is unhealthy or ailing <note>original: *der die das kranckliche / nie-zdrowy*</note>. Adjective, 3rd class.

295 **Fasting** <note>original: *Jejunus*</note>, one who is hungry or has an empty stomach <note>original: *der die das nüchterne / głodny*</note>. Adjective, 3rd class.
**To desire** <note>original: *Appeto*</note>, to be eager for or to crave <note>original: *begierig seyn / pozadam*</note>. Verb, 3rd conjugation, Active.
**Nourishment** <note>original: *Alimentum*</note>, food or sustenance <note>original: *die Nahrung / pokarm*</note>. Neuter noun, 2nd declension <note>The OCR notes '3', but Alimentum is typically 2nd declension.</note>.

296 **Satiated** <note>original: *Satur*</note>, one who is full or satisfied <note>original: *der die das Satte / nascony*</note>. Adjective, 3rd class.
**To loathe** <note>original: *Fastidio*</note>, to reject with disgust or to feel an aversion to food <note>original: *verwerffen / eckeln / brzydze sie*</note>. Verb, 4th conjugation, Active.

297 **Delicate** <note>original: *Delicatus*</note>, one who is tender, pampered, or dainty <note>original: *der die das zärtliche / ukochany*</note>. Adjective, 3rd class.
**To select** <note>original: *Deligo*</note>, to choose out or pick <note>original: *außwehlen / wybieram*</note>. Verb, 3rd conjugation, Active.

298 **Healthy** <note>original: *Sanus*</note>, one who is sound in body or mind <note>original: *der die das gesunde / zdrowy*</note>. Adjective, 3rd class.
**Well** <note>original: *Benè*</note>, in a good or right manner <note>original: *gut / wol / dobrze*</note>. Adverb of quality.
**To be strong** <note>original: *Valeo*</note>, to be in good health or be vigorous <note>original: *wol auff seyn / zdrowem*</note>. Verb, 2nd conjugation, Neuter.

299 **Ill** <note>original: *Ægrotus*</note>, a sick person <note>original: *krancke / chory*</note>. Adjective, 3rd class.
**To be sick** <note>original: *Ægroto*</note>, to be unwell or languish <note>original: *krancken / unbaß seyn / choruie*</note>. Verb, 1st conjugation, Neuter.

300 **To ache** <note>original: *Doleo*</note>, to feel pain or suffer <note>original: *schmerzen / boleie*</note>. Verb, 2nd conjugation, Neuter.
**To complain** <note>original: *Queror*</note>, to lament or bewail one's condition <note>original: *sich beklagen / uskarzam sie*</note>. Verb, 3rd conjugation, Deponent.

301 **Disease** <note>original: *Morbus*</note>, a sickness or malady <note>original: *die Kranckheit / choroba*</note>. Masculine noun, 2nd declension.
**Without** <note>original: *Sine*</note>, apart from or lacking <note>original: *ohne / okrom / bez*</note>. Preposition governing the ablative case.
**Pain** <note>original: *Dolor*</note>, suffering or physical ache <note>original: *der Schmerze / bolesc*</note>. Masculine noun, 3rd declension.
**Breath** <note>original: *Anhelitus*</note>, gasping, panting, or the act of breathing <note>original: *das keuchen / dyßenie*</note>. Masculine noun, 4th declension.

<meta>This page continues the catalog of physical states, moving from the rest and wakefulness discussed on the previous page to the conditions of the body regarding health, appetite, and illness. It follows a logical progression from the sensation of hunger to the state of being satiated, and finally into the realm of sickness and physical pain. ## OF THE SICKLY.

295 A fasting person <note>original: *Jejun⁹*</note> desires nourishment;

296 One who is full <note>original: *Satūr*</note> loathes food <note>original: *escas*</note>.

297 A delicate person <note>original: *Delicātus*</note> picks and chooses:

298 A healthy person is very well:

299 A sick person is ill.

300 He who feels pain, complains:

301 Illnesses without pain, and with shortness of breath <note>original: *anhelitu*</note>.

<column-break/>

## Of the Sickly.

A fasting person <note>original: *Ein Nüchterner*</note> is eager for nourishment;

A satisfied person <note>original: *Ein Satter*</note> rejects food <note>original: *verwerfft die Speisen*</note> (is disgusted by food).

A pampered person <note>original: *Ein Zärtling*</note> picks and chooses:

A healthy person <note>original: *Ein Gesunder*</note> is well:

A sick person is sickly <note>original: *Ein Krancker kräncket*</note> (is unwell).

He who is in pain, laments.

The illnesses without pain, and with gasping <note>original: *keuchen*</note>.

<column-break/>

## OF THE UNHEALTHY, the sick.

The hungry person desires food.

The satiated person <note>original: *Nasycony*</note> is repulsed by dishes.

The pampered one <note>original: *Ukochány*</note> (the favorite) chooses:

the healthy one <note>original: *Zdrowy*</note> is doing well:

The sick person is ailing.

When something hurts, he complains.

Illnesses besides pains and with shortness of breath <note>original: *zdyzchawica*</note> (oppression).

**Sickly** <note>original: *Morbidos*</note>, those who are unhealthy or prone to disease <note>original: *Der Kräncklichen / Nie-zdrowych*</note>. Adjective, masculine plural.

295 **Fasting** <note>original: *Jejunus*</note>, one who has not eaten or is hungry <note>original: *Nüchterner / Głodny*</note>. Adjective, 3rd class.
**To desire** <note>original: *Appeto*</note>, to seek after or have an appetite for <note>original: *begierig seyn / pożąda*</note>. Verb, 3rd conjugation.

296 **Full** <note>original: *Satur*</note>, satiated or satisfied <note>original: *Satter / Nasycony*</note>. Adjective, 3rd class.
**To loathe** <note>original: *Fastidio*</note>, to feel disgust or aversion, especially toward food <note>original: *verwerfft / eckeln / brzydze sie*</note>. Verb, 4th conjugation.

297 **Delicate** <note>original: *Delicatus*</note>, a pampered or fastidious person <note>original: *Zärtling / Ukochány*</note>. In this context, it refers to a "picky eater" or someone accustomed to luxury.

298 **Healthy** <note>original: *Sanus*</note>, sound in body or health <note>original: *Gesunder / Zdrowy*</note>. Adjective, 3rd class.

299 **Sick** <note>original: *Aegrotus*</note>, one who is currently ill <note>original: *Krancker / Chory*</note>.

300 **Pain** <note>original: *Dolor*</note>, physical suffering or ache <note>original: *schmerzet / boli*</note>. Masculine noun, 3rd declension.

301 **Shortness of breath** <note>original: *Anhelitus*</note>, gasping, wheezing, or difficulty breathing <note>original: *keuchen / zdyzchawica*</note>. Masculine noun, 4th declension.

310 **Recent** or **Fresh** <note>original Latin: *Recens, tis*</note>, the fresh <note>original German: *der die das frische*</note> / fresh <note>original Polish: *świeży*</note>. *adjective, category 1.*
**Wound** <note>original Latin: *Vulnus, eris*</note>, the wound <note>original German: *die Wunde*</note> / wound <note>original Polish: *rana*</note>. *Noun, 3rd declension.*
**Heal** <note>original Latin: *Sano, ávi, átum, áre*</note>, to heal <note>original German: *heilen*</note> / I heal or restore <note>original Polish: *lecze uzdrawiam*</note>. *1st conjugation, Active.*

311 **Rot** <note>original Latin: *Putreo, ui, ére*</note>, to rot or fester <note>original German: *faulen*</note> / it rots <note>original Polish: *gnije*</note>. *2nd conjugation, Neuter.*
**Stink** <note>original Latin: *Sordeo, ui, ére*</note>, to be filthy or stink <note>original German: *stincken*</note> / I stink <note>original Polish: *śmierdze*</note>. *2nd conjugation, Neuter.*
**Dung** <note>original Latin: *Fimus, i*</note>, the manure <note>original German: *der Mist*</note> / dung <note>original Polish: *gnoy*</note>. *Masculine, 2nd declension.*
**Corpse** <note>original Latin: *Cadâver, veris*</note>, the carrion <note>original German: *das Aaß*</note> / carcass or corpse <note>original Polish: *ścierźw / trup*</note>. *Neuter, 3rd declension.*

312 **Robust** or **Strong** <note>original Latin: *Robustus, a, um*</note>, the strong <note>original German: *der die das starcke*</note> / strong <note>original Polish: *mocny*</note>. *adjective, 3 endings.*
**Can** or **To be able** <note>original Latin: *Possum, potui, posse*</note>, to be able <note>original German: *können*</note> / I can <note>original Polish: *moge*</note>. *irregular verb.*
**Many** or **Much** <note>original Latin: *Multus, a, um*</note>, the many <note>original German: *der die das viele*</note> / many <note>original Polish: *wiele*</note>. *adjective, category 3.*
**Carry** or **Bear** <note>original Latin: *Fero, tuli, latum, ferre*</note>, to bear <note>original German: *tragen*</note> / I carry <note>original Polish: *niośe*</note>. *irregular active verb.*

313 **Tender** or **Weak** <note>original Latin: *Tener, a, um*</note>, the delicate <note>original German: *der die das zarte*</note> / weak <note>original Polish: *słaby*</note>. *adjective, category 3.*
**Little** or **Not enough** <note>original Latin: *Parùm*</note>, little <note>original German: *wenig*</note> / little <note>original Polish: *mało*</note>. *adverb of quantity.*
**Endure** or **Suffer** <note>original Latin: *Suffero, sustuli, sublatum, sufferre*</note>, to endure <note>original German: *ertragen*</note> / I endure <note>original Polish: *znośe*</note>. *3rd conjugation, irregular active.*

314 **Blind** <note>original Latin: *Cæcus, a, um*</note>, the blind <note>original German: *der die das blinde*</note> / blind <note>original Polish: *ślepy*</note>. *adjective, 3 endings.*
**Not** <note>original Latin: *Non*</note>, not <note>original German: *nicht*</note> / not <note>original Polish: *nie*</note>. *negative adverb.*
**See** <note>original Latin: *Video, vidi, visum, vidére*</note>, to see <note>original German: *sehen*</note> / I see <note>original Polish: *widzę*</note>. *2nd conjugation, Active.*

315 **Deaf** <note>original Latin: *Surdus, a, um*</note>, the deaf <note>original German: *taube*</note> / deaf <note>original Polish: *głuchy*</note>. *adjective, category 3.*
**Hear** <note>original Latin: *Audio, ívi, ítum, ire*</note>, to hear <note>original German: *hören*</note> / I hear <note>original Polish: *słyße*</note>. *4th conjugation, Active.*

316 **Mute** or **Dumb** <note>original Latin: *Mutus, a, um*</note>, the mute <note>original German: *der die das stumme*</note> / mute <note>original Polish: *niemy*</note>. *adjective, 3 endings.*
**Speak** <note>original Latin: *Loquor, locutus sum, loqui*</note>, to talk <note>original German: *reden*</note> / I speak <note>original Polish: *mowie*</note>. *3rd conjugation, <term>deponent</term>* <note>a verb that has a passive form but an active meaning</note>.

317 **Touch** <note>original Latin: *Tactus, ûs*</note>, the sense of feeling <note>original German: *das fühlen*</note> / touching <note>original Polish: *dotykanie*</note>. *Masculine, 4th declension.*
**Lack** or **Want** <note>original Latin: *Careo, ui, itum, ére*</note>, to be without or lack <note>original German: *mangeln*</note> / it is lacking <note>original Polish: *niedostaie*</note>. *2nd conjugation, Neuter.*
**No one** <note>original Latin: *Nemo, inis*</note>, nobody <note>original German: *niemand*</note> / no one <note>original Polish: *żaden*</note>. *Common gender, 3rd declension.*

**Recent, Wound, Heal, Rot, Stink, Dung, Corpse, Strong, Can, Many, Carry, Tender, Little, Endure, Blind, See, Deaf, Hear, Mute, Speak, Touch, Lack, No one**

<meta>This page continues the instructional sequence on human health and the body. Having previously discussed specific illnesses like fevers and strokes, it now moves to general physiological truths: the healing of wounds, the nature of bodily decay, and the primary senses. The catchword "Niß" (likely from the Latin *Nisi*, meaning "unless") prepares the reader for a conditional statement on the following page.</meta>

| English (from Latin) | English (from German) | English (from Polish) |
|-------|--------|--------|
| 310 A **fresh** <note>original: *recens*</note> wound is healed | A fresh wound is healed. | A fresh wound is healed. |
| 311 Whatever rots, stinks like **dung** <note>original: *fimus*</note> and a **corpse** <note>original: *cadaver*</note>. | Whatever rots / that stinks like **manure** and **carrion**. | Whatever rots / stinks like dung and **carrion** <note>original: *ścierw*</note>. |
| 312 A **robust** <note>original: *robustus*</note> person can carry much: | A **strong** one can carry much / | A **powerful (strong)** one can carry much / |
| 313 A **delicate** <note>original: *tener*</note> person can endure little. | A **tender** one can endure little. | A **weak** one can endure little. |
| 314 The **blind** person does not see: | A **blind** man sees not: | The **blind** man does not see. |
| 315 The **deaf** person does not hear: | A **deaf** man hears not: | The **deaf** man does not hear. |
| 316 The **mute** person does not speak. | A **mute** man speaks not: | The **mute** man does not speak. |
| 317 No one lacks the sense of **touch** <note>original: *tactu*</note>. | The sense of **feeling** is lacking in no one. | **Touching** is lacking in none <unclear>nieſtrawa</unclear>. |

### Vocabulary Definitions

**Wound** <note>Latin: *vulnus*; German: *wunde*; Polish: *rana*</note> — a physical injury to the body's tissue.

**Is healed** <note>Latin: *sanatur*</note> — the process of returning to health or restoring the integrity of the skin.

**Corpse** <note>Latin: *cadaver*</note> — a dead body, often used here to imply the foul smell of organic decay.

**Strong or Robust** <note>Latin: *robustus*</note> — referring to physical vigor and the capacity for labor.

**Blind** <note>Latin: *caecus*; German: *blinder*; Polish: *slepy*</note> — one who is deprived of the sense of sight.

**Deaf** <note>Latin: *surdus*; German: *tauber*; Polish: *głuchy*</note> — one who is deprived of the sense of hearing.

**Mute** <note>Latin: *mutus*; German: *stummer*; Polish: *niemy*</note> — one who is unable to speak.

**Touch** <note>Latin: *tactu*</note> — the most fundamental sense, which the author notes is present in all living people, regardless of other disabilities.

<summary>This page outlines basic physical conditions of the human body, contrasting health and strength with decay and sensory impairments. It concludes by identifying touch as the most universal and essential of the human senses.</summary>
<keywords>healing, wounds, decay, physical strength, blindness, deafness, muteness, sense of touch, physiology</keywords>

<margin>332</margin> **Shepherd** <note>original: *Pastor*</note>, the **herdsman** <note>original: *der Hirt*</note> / **shepherd** <note>original: *pasterz*</note> <term>M. 3.</term> <note>Masculine, 3rd declension</note>

# **Hunter, Fowler, Fisherman, Cook, Boiling, Perch, Salmon**

338 **Hunter** <note>original Latin: *Venátor*; German: *der Jäger*; Polish: *łowiec*</note>, a masculine noun of the third declension.
**To hunt** <note>original Latin: *Venor*; German: *jagen*; Polish: *łowie* or *chwytam*</note>, a deponent verb of the first conjugation.
**Wild** <note>original Latin: *Ferus*; German: *wild*; Polish: *dziki*</note>, an adjective of three endings.

339 **Fowler** <note>original Latin: *Auceps*; German: *der Vogelſteller* (one who sets traps for birds); Polish: *ptaßnik*</note>, a masculine noun of the third declension.
**To catch** or **take** <note>original Latin: *Capio*; German: *fangen*; Polish: *łowie*</note>, an active verb of the third conjugation.
**Bird** <note>original Latin: *Vólucris*; German: *der Vogel*; Polish: *ptak*</note>, a feminine noun of the third declension.

340 **Fisherman** <note>original Latin: *Piſcátor*; German: *der Fiſcher*; Polish: *ribitw* or *rybak*</note>, a masculine noun of the third declension.
**To fish** <note>original Latin: *Piſcor*; German: *fiſchen*; Polish: *łowie ryby*</note>, a deponent verb of the first conjugation.
**Net** <note>original Latin: *Rete*; German: *das Netze*; Polish: *ſieć*</note>, a neuter noun of the third declension.
**Fish-pond** <note>original Latin: *Piſcina*; German: *der Teich*; Polish: *ſadzawká*</note>, a feminine noun of the first declension.

341 **Cook** <note>original Latin: *Coquus*; German: *der Koch*; Polish: *kucharz*</note>, a masculine noun of the second declension.
**Above** or **over** <note>original Latin: *Super*; German: *über*; Polish: *na* or *nad*</note>, a preposition governing the accusative and ablative cases.
**Hearth** <note>original Latin: *Focus*; German: *der Herd*; Polish: *ogniſko*</note>, a masculine noun of the second declension.
**To prepare** <note>original Latin: *Paro*; German: *bereiten*; Polish: *gotuie*</note>, an active verb of the first conjugation.
**Food** <note>original Latin: *Cibus*; German: *die Speiſe*; Polish: *pokarm*</note>, a masculine noun of the second declension.

342 **To boil** or **stew** <note>original Latin: *Elixo*; German: *ſieden*; Polish: *warze*</note>, an active verb of the first conjugation.
**Cauldron** or **kettle** <note>original Latin: *Ahénum*; German: *der Keſſel*; Polish: *kocioł*</note>, a neuter noun of the second declension.
**Copper** <note>original Latin: *Cupreus*; German: *kupfferne*; Polish: *miedziany*</note>, an adjective of three endings.

**Bleak** or **Whitefish** <note>original Latin: *Alburnus*; German: *der Weißfiſch*; Polish: *płocicá*</note>, a masculine noun of the second declension.
**Eel** <note>original Latin: *Agvilla*; German: *der Aal*; Polish: *węgorz*</note>, a feminine noun of the first declension.
**Barbel** <note>original Latin: *Barbus*; German: *die Barme*; Polish: *barwená*</note>, a masculine noun of the second declension.
**Crayfish** <note>original Latin: *Cancer*; German: *der Krebs*; Polish: *rak*</note>, a masculine noun of the second declension.
**Carp** <note>original Latin: *Cyprinus*; German: *die Karpffe*; Polish: *karp*</note>, a masculine noun of the second declension.
**Minnow** <note>original Latin: *Foxinus*; German: *die Elriße*; Polish: *wegorzycá*</note>, a masculine noun of the second declension.
**Sea Gudgeon** <note>original Latin: *Fundulus*; German: *der Gründling*; Polish: *kiełb morſki*</note>, a masculine noun of the second declension.
**River Gudgeon** <note>original Latin: *Gobius*; German: *der Gründling*; Polish: *kiełb naſki*</note>, a masculine noun of the second declension.
**Perch** <note>original Latin: *Perca*; German: *der Perſche*; Polish: *okuń*</note>, a feminine noun of the first declension.
**Salmon** <note>original Latin: *Salmo*; German: *der Lachs*; Polish: *łoſoś*</note>, a masculine noun of the third declension.

<meta>The previous page concluded the discussion on food preparation, specifically seasoning and roasting meats. This page transitions into beverages—focusing on the storage and fermentation of wine and beer—and then moves into the logistics of early modern transportation, beginning with the wagoner and his team. The catchword "When" (original Latin: *Quum*) at the bottom indicates the beginning of the next section on the wagoner's journey.</meta>

| | | |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 352 We store the drink in the cellar. | We keep the beverage in the cellar. | We store the drink in the cellar. |
| 353 Wine is made from grapes: | From the grapes comes the wine | From clusters of grapes comes wine: |
| 354 When the dregs settle. | When the yeast settles. | It settles upon the dregs. |
| 355 Beer is drawn from the barrel; | The beer is tapped from the cask; | They draw beer from the barrel. |
| 356 Into flasks and jars. | Into bottles and buckets. | Into bottles and buckets. |
| 357 The wagoner hitches the horses: | The carter harnesses the horses: | The driver hitches the horses. |
| 358 And greases the wheels: | And oils the wheels: | And greases the wheels: |
| 359 And sets off quickly: | And drives swiftly: | And travels fast: |
| 360 But he sticks in the mud; | But he gets stuck in the mire: | Or he gets bogged down in the mud. |

<margin>352</margin> **Drink** <note>original Latin: *potum*; German: *Tranck*; Polish: *nápoj*</note> A general term for any beverage, often used here in the context of fermented drinks.
**Cellar** <note>original Latin: *cellâ*; German: *Keller*; Polish: *piwnicá*</note> An underground room used for keeping provisions cool.
<margin>353</margin> **Wine** <note>original Latin: *vinum*; German: *Wein*; Polish: *wino*</note> Specifically fermented grape juice.
<margin>354</margin> **Dregs** <note>original Latin: *fecibus*; German: *Häfen*; Polish: *drożdże*</note> The sediment (yeast and grape solids) that falls to the bottom of the vessel during fermentation.
<margin>355</margin> **Beer** <note>original Latin: *cerevisia*; German: *Bier*; Polish: *piwo*</note> A grain-based fermented beverage. 
**Barrel** <note>original Latin: *dolio*; German: *Fasse*; Polish: *beczki*</note> A large wooden vessel for storing liquids.
<margin>356</margin> **Flasks** <note>original Latin: *lagénas*; German: *Flaschen*; Polish: *flásze*</note> Narrow-necked vessels for transport.
**Jars** <note>original Latin: *amphoras*; German: *Eymer*; Polish: *wiádrá*</note> Large vessels or buckets for carrying larger quantities.
<margin>357</margin> **Wagoner** <note>original Latin: *auríga*; German: *Fuhrmann*; Polish: *woźnicá*</note> A driver of a horse-drawn vehicle.
<margin>358</margin> **Wheels** <note>original Latin: *rotas*; German: *Räder*; Polish: *koła*</note> To "grease the wheels" was a literal necessity in the early modern period to prevent friction and fire in wooden axles.
<margin>360</margin> **Mud** <note>original Latin: *coeno*; German: *Koth*; Polish: *błocie*</note> A common hazard for travelers on unpaved early modern roads.

<meta>This page continues the theme of early modern logistics and transportation introduced on the previous page. Having discussed the wagoner and his team on land, the text now transitions to transport by water—detailing the roles of sailors and oarsmen, the types of vessels used, and the infrastructure of rivers and bridges. It concludes with the departure of a traveler.</meta>

361 **To carry** <note>original: *veho, vexi, vectum, vehere* / *führen* / *wiozę*</note>, to transport or convey.
**A burden** <note>original: *onus, eris* / *die Lastwaare* / *ciężar*</note>, referring to heavy goods or a load.

362 **A wagon** <note>original: *currus, ûs* / *der Wagen* / *woz*</note>, a wheeled vehicle for transport.
**A sledge** <note>original: *traha, æ* / *der Schlitten* / *sanie*</note>, used for travel over snow or ice.

363 **A sailor** <note>original: *nauta, æ* / *der Schiffmann* / *szyper*</note>, or boatman.
**To sail** <note>original: *navigo, ávi, átum, áre* / *schiffen* / *żegluię / iadę po wodzie*</note>, to travel by water.
**A ship** <note>original: *navis, is* / *das Schiff* / *okret*</note>, a general term for a sea-faring vessel.

364 **A helmsman** <note>original: *nauclérus, i* / *der Stewermann* / *sternik*</note>, the person who pilots the ship.
**To steer** <note>original: *guberno, ávi, átum, áre* / *stewren* / *kieruię / steruie / rządzę*</note>, to direct the course or govern the movement of the vessel.

365 **An oarsman** <note>original: *remex, migis* / *der Ruderknecht* / *rudelnik / powoźnik*</note>, a rower who provides propulsion.
**To row** <note>original: *remigo, ávi, átum, áre* / *rudern* / *wiosłem robię*</note>, to move a boat through the water with oars.

366 **Through** <note>original: *per* / *durch* / *przez*</note>, a preposition indicating passage.
**A river** <note>original: *fluvius, i* / *der Wasserfluß* / *rzeká*</note>, a flowing body of water.
**To wade** <note>original: *vado, ávi, átum, áre* / *waten* / *brodzę*</note>, to cross a river or stream on foot.

367 **To use** <note>original: *utor, usus sum, uti* / *gebrauchen* / *używám*</note>, or to make use of.
**A skiff** <note>original: *scapha, æ* / *der Kahn / die Nache* / *łodź / czołn / skutá*</note>, a small boat, barge, or canoe.

368 **To build** <note>original: *struo, xi, ctum, ere* / *bawen* / *buduię*</note>, or to construct.
**A bridge** <note>original: *pons, tis* / *die Brücke* / *most*</note>.
**A footbridge** <note>original: *ponticulus, i* / *der Steg* / *mostek / podstáwek*</note>, or a small walkway.

369 **A traveler** <note>original: *viator, óris* / *der Wandersmann* / *podrożnik*</note>, a person on a journey.
**To depart** <note>original: *egredior, egressus sum, égredi* / *außgehen* / *wychodzę*</note>, to go out or set forth.
**Travel money** <note>original: *viaticum, i* / *der Zehrpfennig* / *nadrożne*</note>, the funds or provisions kept for a journey.

<sig>I go.</sig> <note>Latin: *Eo.*</note>

--- Page 103 ---
<meta>This page transitions from land-based logistics to water transport and travel preparations. It details the various roles on a vessel—from the captain to the rowers—and the infrastructure used to cross rivers, such as skiffs and bridges, concluding with the departure of a traveler.</meta>

### Ic

| | | |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 361 When he carries heavy loads: | When he transports cargo: | When he carries a weight: |
| 362 By wagon or by sledge. | On a wagon or a sledge. | On a wagon or on a sledge. |
| 363 The sailor sails in a ship: | The boatman sails in a ship: | The skipper sails in a ship (or boat): |
| 364 The helmsman steers: | The steersman guides the way: | The pilot directs the course. |
| 365 The oarsmen row: | The rowers pull the oars: | The oarsmen work with the oars: |
| 366 We wade through the flowing rivers | We wade through the water-currents. | We ford through the rivers. |
| 367 Or we use skiffs. | Or we use small boats (punts). | Or we use canoes. |
| 368 Or we build bridges and footbridges: | Or we build bridges and walkways. | Or we build bridges and small crossings (planks). |
| 369 The traveler sets out with travel money. | The wanderer goes out with his travel allowance. | The traveler departs with money for the journey. |

361 **Heavy loads** <note>original: *onera* / *Lastwaaren* / *cieżar*</note>, the goods or cargo being transported by a carrier.

362 **A wagon** <note>original: *currus* / *Wagen* / *woz*</note>, a wheeled vehicle, and **a sledge** <note>original: *traha* / *Schlitten* / *sanie*</note>, a vehicle without wheels for sliding over ice, snow, or rough ground.

363 **A sailor** <note>original: *nauta* / *Schiffmann* / *szyper*</note>, one who travels by water in a **ship** <note>original: *navis* / *Schiffe* / *okręt*</note>.

364 **A helmsman** <note>original: *nauclérus* / *Stewermañ* / *sternik*</note>, the person in charge of the vessel's direction. To **steer** <note>original: *gubernat* / *stewert* / *kieruie*</note> is to control the rudder or helm.

365 **Oarsmen** <note>original: *remiges* / *Ruderknechte* / *rudelnicy*</note>, those who provide manual propulsion. To **row** <note>original: *remigant* / *rudern* / *wiosłami robią*</note> is to move the boat using long wooden oars.

366 **To wade** <note>original: *vadámus* / *waten* / *brodzimy*</note>, to cross a **river** <note>original: *fluvius* / *Wassersflüsse* / *rzeki*</note> on foot where the water is shallow enough.

367 **Skiffs** <note>original: *scaphis* / *Kähne* / *czółnow*</note>, small, light boats often used for short distances or in shallow water.

368 **Bridges** <note>original: *pontes* / *Brücken* / *mosty*</note> and **footbridges** <note>original: *ponticulos* / *Stege* / *ławki*</note>, structures erected to allow safe passage over water or obstacles.

369 **A traveler** <note>original: *viátor* / *Wandersman* / *podrożny*</note>, anyone on a journey. **Travel money** <note>original: *viaticum* / *zehrpfennig* / *pieniądze w drogę*</note> refers to the funds, provisions, or "journey-pennies" saved for the costs of a trip.

<summary>This page outlines the terminology for maritime transport and river crossings, including the roles of sailors and helmsmen, and concludes with the departure of a traveler provided with travel money.</summary>
<keywords>sailing, navigation, rowing, rivers, bridges, travel money, logistics, early modern transport</keywords>

--- Page 104 ---
<meta>This page continues the theme of travel from the previous page, transitioning from the physical act of movement and its hazards (paths, leaps, and pits) to the figurative and literal "paths" of commerce and trade.</meta>

370 **To go** <note>original: *Eo, ivi, itum, ire* / *gehen* / *idę*</note> <term>anomalous intransitive verb</term>.
**Without** <note>original: *Sine* / *ohn* / *okrom*</note> <term>preposition governing the ablative case</term>.
**Footpath** <note>original: *Semita* / *der Fußsteig* / *ścieżka*</note> <term>feminine noun, 1st declension</term>.
**Leap** or **Jump** <note>original: *Saltus* / *der Sprung* / *skok*</note> <term>masculine noun, 4th declension</term>.

371 **To beware** or **To take care** <note>original: *Caveo, vi, cautum, ére* / *sich hütten* / *ochraniam* / *wáruie*</note> <term>2nd conjugation active verb</term>.
**Winding ways** or **Digressions** <note>original: *Ambáges* / *die umbschweiffe* / *krązenie*</note> <term>feminine noun, 3rd declension, plural only</term>.
**Swamp** or **Pool** <note>original: *Palus, ūdis* / *der Pfuhl* / *bagnisko*</term> <term>feminine noun, 3rd declension</term>.

372 **Lest** or **So that... not** <note>original: *Ne* / *daß nicht* / *áby nie*</note> <term>causal conjunction</term>.
**To wander** or **To stray** <note>original: *Erro, ávi, átum, áre* / *jrren* / *błądze*</note> <term>1st conjugation intransitive verb</term>.
**To fall** <note>original: *Cado, cecidi, casum, dere* / *fallen* / *padam* / *upádam*</note> <term>3rd conjugation intransitive verb</term>.
**Pit** or **Ditch** <note>original: *Fovea* / *die Grube* / *dół*</note> <term>feminine noun, 1st declension</term>.

373 **To yield** or **To withdraw** <note>original: *Cedo, cessi, cessum, dere* / *weichen* / *ustępuie* / *uchodzę*</note> <term>3rd conjugation intransitive verb</term>.
**Backward** <note>original: *Retrò* / *zurücke* / *na zad*</note> <term>adverb of place</term>.
**To be unable** <note>original: *Nequeo, ivi, itum, ire* / *nicht können* / *niemogę*</note> <term>anomalous intransitive verb</term>.
**To proceed** or **To go forward** <note>original: *Pergo, perréxi, perrectum, ere* / *fort gehen* / *postępuie* / *idę*</note> <term>3rd conjugation intransitive verb</term>.

374 **Merchant** <note>original: *Mercátor, óris* / *der Kauffmann* / *kupiec*</note> <term>masculine noun, 3rd declension</term>.
**To trade** or **To handle** <note>original: *Tracto, ávi, átum, áre* / *handeln* / *bawię się* / *spráwuie*</note> <term>1st conjugation active verb</term>.
**Merchandise** or **Goods** <note>original: *Merx, mércis* / *die Waare* / *towar*</note> <term>feminine noun, 3rd declension</term>.

375 **To fear** <note>original: *Metuo, ui, ere* / *fürchten* / *boię się*</note> <term>3rd conjugation active verb</term>.
**Loss** or **Damage** <note>original: *Damnum, i* / *der Schaden* / *Verlust* / *śkoda*</note> <term>neuter noun, 2nd declension</term>.

376 **To seek** <note>original: *Quæro, sivi, itum, ere* / *suchen* / *szukam*</note> <term>3rd conjugation active verb</term>.
**Profit** or **Gain** <note>original: *Lucrum, i* / *der Gewinn* / *zysk*</note> <term>neuter noun, 2nd declension</term>.

378 **Coin** <note>original: Monéta</note> is carried in a **purse** (pouch)
379 Since with **money**,
380 We pay what we **owe** (**debts**).
381 We **buy** and **sell**,
382 **Dearly** <note>at a high price</note> or **cheaply** (at a great or small price)
383 **Pennies**, **groschen**, and **guilders** <note>original: nummi, grossi & floréni; these represent the standard small, medium, and large denominations of the early modern period</note> are various:

The **coin** is carried in the **pouch**:
Because with the **money**
We pay /
what we **owe** (the **debts**)
We **buy** and **sell** /
**Dearly** or **cheaply**.
The **pennies**, **groschen**, and **guilders** are different:
Each one in its **value**.
The **spinner** at the **distaff** spins from the **flax**.

The **coin** (money) is usually carried in a **purse** (pouch):
Since with **money** the **debts** /
which we **owe** we **pay**.
We **buy** and **sell**:
**Dearly** or **cheaply**.
**Small coins** (change), **groschen**, and **gold pieces** are diverse:
Each in its **price**.
The **spinner** at the **distaff** spins from **flax**.

**Vocabulary Guide**

**The Carpenter** <note>original: Lignarius; Zimmerman; Cieśla</note> — A craftsman who works with timber.
**The Stonemason** <note>original: Murarius; Mäurer; Murarz</note> — A builder who works with stone or brick.
**The Glazier** <note>original: Vitriarius; Gläser; Szklarz</note> — A specialist who cuts and sets glass for windows.
**The Cooper** <note>original: vietor; Bötticher; bednarz</note> — A craftsman who makes wooden staves into barrels, casks, and tubs.
**The Building** <note>original: ædificium</note> — Any large structure under construction.
**The Trade or Craft** <note>original: opificium</note> — Specifically the manual work or labor of a skilled craftsman.
**The Workshop** <note>original: officina</note> — The place where a master craftsman and his apprentices work.

<meta>This page continues an educational linguistic guide. While the previous page discussed the physical crafts of carpentry and masonry, this section moves toward structural integrity (strength versus weakness) and introduces "Impersonal Verbs"—verbs used without a specific personal subject (like "it is pleasing" or "it is fitting"). The catchword "Ado-" from the previous page suggests a section on "Adolescentia" (Youth) may follow later, but here the focus is on moral and physical stability.</meta>

| <note>Original: Latin</note> | <note>Original: German</note> | <note>Original: Polish</note> |
|:---|:---|:---|
| 405 The weak is not firm nor perfect: | That which is weak is not firm, nor is it complete: | The weak is not strong nor perfect: |
| 406 It is propped up with supports; | It is supported with props; | It was supported by props; |
| 407 So that it does not fall. | So that it does not fall down (or collapse). | So that it should not fall. |
| ### IMPERSONAL VERBS. | ### IMPERSONAL VERBS. | ### IMPERSONAL VERBS. |
| 408 If it is pleasing to you, it is permitted: | Where it is pleasing to you, it stands free <note>meaning: it is allowed</note>: | If something is pleasing to you, it is permitted: |
| 409 If you are weary of labor or a good deed, you should be ashamed: | Where the work or the good deed tires you, then be ashamed: | Shame on you if you are lazy toward work and good deeds: |
| 410 It is fitting for you to repent for sin and discord: | You should feel regret for sins and for disunity. | It belongs to you that you should regret (do penance) for sins and for discord. |

<term>Impersonal Verbs</term> <note>These are verbs that do not have a person (I, you, he) as a subject, but instead use an implied "it." In Latin, these often describe feelings or legal permissions, such as "it shames me" or "it is allowed."</note>

<note>Vocabulary from this section: **Weak** (Debile), **supports** (fulcris), **Impersonal verbs** (Impersonalia), **it is pleasing** (libet), **it is permitted** (licet), **it wearies** (piget), **let it shame** (pudeat), **It is fitting** (Decet), **to repent** (pœnitére), **of sin** (peccáti), **of discord** (discordiæ).</note>

**Come now** <note>original Latin: *Agedum*; Polish: *nuż tedy*</note>. An interjection used to urge someone to action.
**Let us wait** <note>original Latin: *expectemus*; Polish: *oczekawaymy*</note>. The Latin text uses the abbreviation *expectém⁹* where the symbol *⁹* represents the "-us" ending.
**Arrival** <note>original Latin: *adventus*</note>. A formal word for someone's coming or reaching a destination.
**Neighborhood** <note>original Latin: *viciniâ*; German: *Nachbarſchafft*</note>. Refers to the immediate surrounding area or vicinity.
**Portico / Gallery / Porch** <note>original Latin: *porticum*; German: *Läuben*; Polish: *podśionek*</note>. These terms describe a covered walkway or entrance common in early modern architecture. The German *Läuben* <note>modern: *Laube*</note> specifically suggests a structure covered with foliage or a colonnade.
**Welcome** <note>German: *willkommen*</note>. Literally "well-come."
**Greetings / Be greeted** <note>original Latin: *Salve*; Polish: *pozdrowion*</note>. A standard formal greeting.
**Small town** <note>Polish: *miasteczká*</note>. A diminutive form suggesting a village or township rather than a major city.

<meta>This page transitions from the conversational phrases found on the previous page into a structured trilingual glossary. While the previous page applied vocabulary within a dialogue about students and their lessons, this page provides the formal dictionary definitions and grammatical classifications for the words being studied. The entries provide equivalents in Latin, German, and Polish, illustrating the multilingual educational environment of the period.</meta>

439 **He, she, it** or **that** <note>original: *Is, ea, id* (Latin); *der die das jen* (German); *tá, to* (Polish)</note>. <term>Relative pronominal adjective</term>.
**It is better** <note>original: *Præstat* (Latin); *es ist besser* (German); *lepiej* (Polish)</note>. <term>1st conjugation, impersonal verb</term>.
**Than** <note>original: *Quam* (Latin); *alß* (German); *niż* (Polish)</note>. <term>Elective conjunction</term>.
**To chatter, gossip, or murmur** <note>original: *Garrio* (Latin); *schwaßen, waschen* (German); *szemrze* (Polish)</note>. <term>4th conjugation, neuter verb</term>.

440 **Certainly** or **indeed** <note>original: *Omninò* (Latin); *freylich* (German); *owßem* (Polish)</note>. <term>Affirmative adverb</term>.
**Timely** or **quickly** <note>original: *Maturè* (Latin); *zeitlich* (German); *w czás, rychło* (Polish)</note>. <term>Adverb of time</term>.

---

441 **Perhaps** <note>original: *Fortassè* (Latin); *vielleicht* (German); *podomno* (Polish)</note>. <term>Adverb of doubt</term>.
**But** or **yet** <note>original: *Sed* (Latin); *doch* (German); *ále* (Polish)</note>. <term>Adversative conjunction</term>.
**Scarcely** or **hardly** <note>original: *Vix* (Latin); *kaum* (German); *lédwie* (Polish)</note>. <term>Remissive adverb</term> <note>An adverb that indicates a lessening of intensity</note>.
**Hey!** or **Look here** <note>original: *Eho* (Latin); *ey lieber* (German); *á iuß* (Polish)</note>. <term>Interjection for calling someone</term>.
**To doubt** <note>original: *Dubito* (Latin); *zweiffeln* (German); *wątpie* (Polish)</note>. <term>1st conjugation, neuter verb</term>.

442 **Far off** or **at a distance** <note>original: *Procul* (Latin); *weit* (German); *dáleko* (Polish)</note>. <term>Adverb of place</term>.
**To hasten** or **hurry** <note>original: *Propero* (Latin); *eylen* (German); *spieße śie* (Polish)</note>. <term>1st conjugation, neuter verb</term>.

---

443 **As** or **just like** <note>original: *Ceu* (Latin); *wie* (German); *iáko* (Polish)</note>. <term>Adverb of similarity</term>.
**Shoemaker** <note>original: *Sutor* (Latin); *der Schuster* (German); *świec [szewc]* (Polish)</note>. <term>3rd declension, masculine noun</term>.
**Then** or **furthermore** <note>original: *Tum* (Latin); *alßdenn, tedy, wiec* (German/Polish equivalents)</note>. <term>Continuative conjunction</term>.

444 **Our** <note>original: *Noster, stra, strum* (Latin); *vnsere* (German); *náß* (Polish)</note>. <term>Possessive pronominal adjective</term>.
**Age** or **lifetime** <note>original: *Ætas* (Latin); *das Alter* (German); *stárość* (Polish)</note>. <term>3rd declension, feminine noun</term>.
**Agility** or **quickness** <note>original: *Agilitas* (Latin); *die Hurtigkeit* (German); *pretkość* (Polish)</note>. <term>3rd declension, feminine noun</term>.

445 **So** or **in this way** <note>original: *Sic* (Latin); *so* (German); *ták* (Polish)</note>. <term>Adverb of quality</term>.
**I say** <note>original: *Ajo* (Latin); *ich sage* (German); *mowie, powiádam* (Polish)</note>. <term>Defective and active verb</term> <note>A "defective" verb is one that lacks certain tenses or forms</note>.
**Yes** or **indeed** <note>original: *Imò* (Latin); *ja* (German); *owßem* (Polish)</note>. <term>Affirmative adverb</term>.

446 **Not** <note>original: *Ne* (Latin); *nicht* (German); *nie* (Polish)</note>. <term>Prohibitive adverb</term>.
**To shout** or **cry out** <note>original: *Clamo* (Latin); *schreyen* (German); *wołam* (Polish)</note>. <term>1st conjugation, neuter verb</term>.
**But yet** or **however** <note>original: *Atqui* (Latin); *doch* (German); *á wßák* (Polish)</note>. <term>Adversative conjunction</term>.
**Alone** or **only** <note>original: *Solus, a, um* (Latin); *allein* (German); *sam, sáma, sámo* (Polish)</note>. <term>Adjective</term>.

| 439 That is better than to chatter. <note>original: "Id præstat quàm garrire"</note> | That is better than gossiping (or chattering). <note>original: "Das ist besser alß schwaßen / (waschen)"</note> | Better that than to murmur. <note>original: "Lepiey to á niż szemrzeć"</note> |
| 440 Indeed. Shall we return in good time? <note>original: "Omninò Redibimusne matúrè?"</note> | Certainly. Shall we also come back in good time? <note>original: "Freylich. Werden wir auch zeitlich wiederkommen?"</note> | And indeed. Shall we return in time? <note>original: "Y owßem. á wczás że sie zwrocimy."</note> |
| 441 Perhaps; but hardly. Ho there, do you doubt it? <note>original: "Fortassè; sed vix. Eho dubitas?"</note> | Perhaps; yet hardly. Hey, dear friend, do you doubt? <note>original: "Vielleicht; doch kaum. Ey lieber zweiffelstu?"</note> | Without a doubt. <note>original: "Bez watpienia" — interestingly, the Polish translator chose "without doubt" where the Latin and German use "perhaps."</note> But hardly; why do you doubt? <note>original: "Ale ledwie; czesmuß watpiß."</note> |
| 442 It is far. We shall hasten. <note>original: "Procul est. Properabimus."</note> | It is far. We want to hurry. <note>original: "Es ist weit. Wir wollen eylen."</note> | It is far. Let us hurry. <note>original: "Dáleko jest. Spießmy sie."</note> |
| 443 Like shoemakers? <note>original: "Ceu sutores?" — likely a reference to a quick, rhythmic pace or a common class comparison.</note> What then? <note>original: "Quid tum?"</note> | Like the shoemakers? What is it more? <note>original: "Wie die Schuster? Was ists mehr?"</note> | Like shoemakers? But what of it? <note>original: "Jáko ßewcy? ále coß nátym."</note> |
| 444 Agility becomes our age. <note>original: "Nostram ætátem decet agilitas."</note> | Quickness suits our age. <note>original: "Vnserm Alter stehet die Hurtigkeit an."</note> | Swiftness belongs to our age. <note>original: "Stárośći náßey przynaleži pretkość."</note> |
| 445 So they say. Nay, so it is. <note>original: "Sic ajunt. Imò sic est."</note> | So they say. Yes, so it is. <note>original: "So sagt man. Ja also ists."</note> | So they say. And indeed it is so. <note>original: "Ták powiádáią. Y owßem ták jest."</note> |
| 446 Do not shout. But we are alone. <note>original: "Ne clama. Atqui sumus soli."</note> | Do not shout, for we are alone. <note>original: "Schrey nicht / sind wir doch allein."</note> | Do not call out, for we are alone. <note>original: "Nie wołay, á wßak sami jestesmy."</note> |

<meta>This page continues the numbered vocabulary list and grammatical exercises. The previous page concluded with a short dialogue about hurrying and social comparisons (like shoemakers). This page shifts into specific vocabulary related to play, time, and the transition into a grammatical explanation of "prepositions" or "propositions."</meta>

447 **How**, <note>original Latin: "Quomodo"</note> *interrogative adverb.*
**I play**, <note>original: "Ludo"</note> *3rd conjugation neuter verb.*

448 **Ball**, <note>original: "Pila"</note> *Feminine noun, 1st declension.*
**Blind Man’s Buff**, <note>original: "Myinda"</note> *Feminine noun, 1st declension.* <note>The German "Blinde Kuh" literally translates to "Blind Cow," a common European name for this game.</note>
**Board game**, <note>original: "Alea"</note> *Feminine noun, 1st declension.* <note>The Polish gloss "warcáby" specifically refers to draughts or checkers.</note>

449 **How long**, <note>original: "Quamdiu"</note> *interrogative adverb.*
450 **The whole**, <note>original: "Totus"</note> *3-termination adjective (masculine, feminine, neuter).*
**Day**, <note>original: "Dies"</note> *Common gender, 3rd declension.* <note>Though usually 5th declension in Classical Latin, the author categorizes it as 3rd here.</note>
**Ho!**, <note>original: "Hui"</note> *Interjection of admiration or surprise.*
**Too much**, <note>original: "Nimius"</note> *3-termination adjective.*
451 **Holidays**, <note>original: "Feriæ"</note> *Feminine noun, 1st declension; used only in the plural.* <note>Refers to feast days or scheduled days of rest.</note>
452 **Would that**, <note>original: "Utinam"</note> *adverb used to express a wish.* <note>Often used in the sense of "God grant" or "if only."</note>
**I refrain**, <note>original: "Abstineo"</note> *2nd conjugation active verb.* <note>To hold oneself back or stay away from.</note>
**From**, <note>original: "A"</note> *preposition governing the ablative case.*
**Malice**, <note>original: "Malitia"</note> *Feminine noun, 1st declension.* <note>Meaning badness, spite, or wickedness.</note>

**Preposition** (or **Purpose**), <note>original: "Præpositio"</note> *Feminine noun, 3rd declension.* <note>The German "Vorsatz" can mean a grammatical preposition, a prefix, or a firm intention/purpose.</note>

453 **I proceed**, <note>original: "Pergo"</note> *3rd conjugation active verb.* <note>Meaning to go on, continue, or move forward.</note>
454 **I ask**, <note>original: "Rogo"</note> *1st conjugation active verb.* <note>To beg, request, or ask.</note>
**Small**, <note>original: "Parvus"</note> *3-termination adjective.*
**A little word**, <note>original: "Vocula"</note> *Feminine noun, 1st declension.* <note>The diminutive form of "vox" (voice/word).</note>
**I explain**, <note>original: "Expóno"</note> *3rd conjugation active verb.* <note>To set forth, expound, or clarify.</note>

| 447 How shall we play? <note>original Latin: "Quomodo ludemus?"</note> | How do we want to play? <note>original German: "Wie wollen wir spielen?"</note> | How will we play? <note>original Polish: "Jakoż grać będziemy?"</note> |
| 448 With the **ball**, at **blind man’s buff**, at the **board game**. <note>The Latin "Myinda" refers to a game of hiding or closing eyes; the German "blinde Kuhe" (Blind Cow) and Polish "zmrużkiem" (blinking/shutting) are regional names for Blind Man's Buff.</note> | With the ball / the blind cow / at the board game. <note>original German: "auff dem Bretspiele"</note> | With the ball / at "blinker" / at checkers. <note>The Polish "warcaby" specifically denotes draughts or checkers.</note> |
| 449 How long? | How long? | How long? |
| 450 The whole day. **Oh!** That is too much. | The whole day. **Huy** / that is too much. | Through the whole day. **Ey**, so excessively. |
| 451 But it is a **holiday**. <note>original Latin: "feriæ"</note> | It is indeed a feast day. | And yet it is a holiday. |
| 452 Would that we might abstain from **malice**. <note>original Latin: "malitiâ"</note> | Would to God / that we held ourselves back from wickedness. <note>original German: "Boßheit"</note> | God grant / that we would leave off malice. <note>original Polish: "złości"</note> |

**463 Against** / **Opposite** <note>Latin: *Adversus*; German: *wider*; Polish: *naprzeciw*</note> — preposition governing the accusative case.
**That one** [masculine, feminine, neuter] <note>Latin: *Ille, illa, illud*; German: *jener/jene/jenes*; Polish: *on/ona/ono*</note> — relative adjective pronoun.
**Impossible** <note>Latin: *Impossibilis, -e*; German: *der/die/das unmögliche*; Polish: *niemożny/niepodobny*</note> — adjective of the second class <note>referring to adjectives with two endings</note>.

**464 At** / **With** <note>Latin: *Apud*; German: *Bey*; Polish: *u*</note> — preposition governing the accusative case.
**Altar** <note>Latin: *Altare, -is*; German: *der Altar*; Polish: *ołtarz*</note> — Neuter noun of the 3rd declension.
**Altar** [likewise] <note>Latin: *Ara, -ae*; German: *item*</note> — Feminine noun of the 1st declension.
**Priest** <note>Latin: *Sacerdos, -otis*; German: *der Priester*; Polish: *kapłan*</note> — Common gender noun of the 3rd declension.

**465 To have** <note>Latin: *Habeo, -ui, -itum, -ere*; German: *haben*; Polish: *mam*</note> — Active verb of the 2nd conjugation.

**Chaplain** / **Deacon** <note>Latin: *Diaconus, -i*; German: *der Kapellan/Diakon*; Polish: *Diakon*</note> — Masculine noun of the 2nd declension.

**466 Rash** / **Thoughtless** <note>Latin: *Temerarius, -a, -um*; German: *fürwitzige/unbesonnene*; Polish: *lekkomyślny*</note> — adjective of the 3rd class <note>referring to adjectives with three endings</note>.
**To mark** / **To note** <note>Latin: *Noto, -avi, -atrum, -are*; German: *mercken*; Polish: *znaczę/pamiętno co czynię*</note> — Active verb of the 1st conjugation.

**467 That** <note>Latin: *Quod*; German: *das*; Polish: *że*</note> — causal conjunction.

**House** <note>Latin: *Aedes, -ium*; German: *das Hauß*; Polish: *dom*</note> — Feminine noun of the 3rd declension, used only in the plural.
**To stay** / **To remain** <note>Latin: *Maneo, mansi, mansum, -ere*; German: *bleiben*; Polish: *zostaję*</note> — Neuter <note>intransitive</note> verb of the 2nd conjugation.

<meta>This page continues the numbered sequence of linguistic exercises. | | | |
|---|---|---|
| 471 What have you done **SECRETLY** [away] from us? <note>original Latin: *CLAM nobis*; German: *heimlich für vns*; Polish: *potájemnie*</note> | What have you done secretly for us? | What have you done to us in secret? |
| 472 Because it is **OPENLY** known to many. <note>original Latin: *PALAM multis*; German: *vielen offenbahr*; Polish: *jawno*</note> | For it is manifest to many. | Because it is public (openly). |
| 473 I have acted **AGAINST** the commandment: <note>original Latin: *CONTRA præceptum*; German: *Wider das Gebot*; Polish: *Naprzeciw przykazániu*</note> | I have acted against the command: | I have done [this] against the commandment. |
| 474 **TOWARD** the teacher I have been very ungrateful. <note>original Latin: *ERGA præceptórem*; German: *Gegen den Lehrmeister*; Polish: *Przeciw náuczycielowi*</note> | I have been very ungrateful toward the master. | I was very ungrateful toward the teacher. |
| 475 A certain man, **ON ACCOUNT OF** his deeds, <note>original Latin: *OB sua facinora*; German: *wegen seiner Thaten*; Polish: *dla swoich spraw*</note> | One, because of his deeds, | Someone, for his own affairs [deeds]. |
| 476 A fugitive **FROM** the house, <note>original Latin: *E domo profugus*; German: *Auß dem Hause flüchtig*; Polish: *Z domu zbieg*</note> | Fleeing out of the house, | A runaway from the house, |
| 477 While he ran **NEAR** the road, <note>original Latin: *JUXTA viam*; German: *neben dem Weg*; Polish: *podle drogi*</note> | While he ran beside the way, | When he ran along the road. |
| 478 He stopped **ON THIS SIDE** of the lake: <note>original Latin: *CITRA (Cis) stagnum*; German: *disseits deß Sees*; Polish: *Z tey strony jeziorá*</note> | He remained on this side of the lake: | He stayed on this side (near) the lake: |

**Vocabulary**

**SECRETLY**, <note>Latin: *CLAM*</note> a preposition used to describe hidden actions.
**OPENLY**, <note>Latin: *PALAM*</note> a preposition describing things done in public view.
**AGAINST**, <note>Latin: *CONTRA*</note> used here for breaking a rule or law.
**TOWARD**, <note>Latin: *ERGA*</note> expressing a relationship or attitude toward another person.
**ON ACCOUNT OF**, <note>Latin: *OB*</note> indicating the reason or cause for something.
**FROM** or **OUT OF**, <note>Latin: *E*</note> indicating movement away from a place.
**NEAR** or **BESIDE**, <note>Latin: *JUXTA*</note> indicating physical proximity.
**ON THIS SIDE OF**, <note>Latin: *CITRA* or *CIS*</note> a spatial preposition.
**Commandment** or **precept**, <note>Latin: *præceptum*</note> a rule given by an authority.
**Deeds** or **crimes**, <note>Latin: *facinora*</note> often refers to significant or notorious acts.
**Fugitive** or **runaway**, <note>Latin: *profugus*</note> someone fleeing from home or justice.
**Lake** or **pool**, <note>Latin: *stagnum*</note> a body of still water.

479 **Thereafter**, then / afterward <note>original Latin: *Deinde*; German: *darnach*; Polish: *potem*</note> *continuing conjunction*
**Beyond**, on the other side / from that side <note>original Latin: *Ultra*; German: *jenseit*; Polish: *z one strone*</note> *preposition ruling the accusative case*
**I advance**, I have advanced, to advance, to continue / I go further / I advance <note>original Latin: *Progredior, progressus sum, progredi*; German: *fortfahren*; Polish: *ide daley, postepuie*</note> 3rd conjugation. *Deponent verb* <note>Deponent verbs have a passive form but an active meaning.</note>

480 **I come**, I came, come, to come, to come / I go / I arrive <note>original Latin: *Venio, veni, ventum, venire*; German: *kommen*; Polish: *ide/ przychodze*</note> 4th conjugation. *Intransitive*
**Desert**, of the desert, the wilderness / wasteland <note>original Latin: *Desertum, i*; German: *die Wüste*; Polish: *puscza*</note> *Neuter noun, 2nd declension*

481 **I go around**, I went around, to go around, to traverse / I circle <note>original Latin: *Obeo, obivi & obii, itum, ire*; German: *vmbgehen*; Polish: *krąże*</note> 4th conjugation type. *Intransitive*
**Around**, about / near <note>original Latin: *Circa*; German: *vmb*; Polish: *około*</note> *preposition ruling the accusative case*
**Swamp**, of the swamp, the marsh / puddle / bog <note>original Latin: *Palus, udis*; German: *der Sumpff*; Polish: *kałuża/ bagnisko*</note> *Feminine noun, 3rd declension*

482 **I wander**, wandered, to wander, to stray / I go around / I wander <note>original Latin: *Oberro, avi, atum, are*; German: *jrren*; Polish: *okrążam / błąkam sie*</note> 1st conjugation. *Intransitive*
**Between**, among / between <note>original Latin: *Inter*; German: *zwischen*; Polish: *miedzy*</note> *preposition ruling the accusative case*
**Grove**, of the grove, the forest / woods <note>original Latin: *Nemus, oris*; German: *der Wald*; Polish: *las*</note> *Neuter noun, 3rd declension*

483 **I sail**, sailed, to sail, to ship / I sail / I travel
by water <note>original Latin: *Navigo, avi, atum, are*; German: *schiffen*; Polish: *żegluie / iade po wodzie*</note> 1st conjugation. *Active*

<meta>This page continues the instructional vocabulary used in a 17th-century classroom setting, following the roles of teachers and students. 536 **The Tutor** <term>original: *Pædagógus* — from the Greek for "child-leader." <meta>This page continues the instructional vocabulary for school life, moving from the teacher’s supervision to the student's behavior and the consequences of laziness. The previous page discussed the roles of the tutor and master; this page details the qualities of a student (diligence versus negligence) and the tools of discipline used in a 17th-century classroom.</meta>

**542** **Diligent**, <note>original: *Diligens*</note> the one who is industrious or hardworking. <note>German: *fleissige*; Polish: *pilny*</note> *Adjective of the 1st declension (third declension group).*
**I make progress**, <note>original: *Proficio*</note> to increase or advance. <note>German: *zunehmen*; Polish: *postepuie*</note> *3rd conjugation, Neuter.*
**Hindrance**, <note>original: *Impedimentum*</note> an obstacle or barrier. <note>German: *das hindernuß*; Polish: *przeszkoda*</note> *Neuter, 2nd declension.*

**543** **Negligent**, <note>original: *Negligens*</note> the one who is lazy or idle. <note>German: *faule*; Polish: *niedbały*</note> *Adjective of the 1st group.*
**I am beaten**, <note>original: *Vapulo*</note> to be struck or punished. <note>German: *geschlagen werden*; Polish: *biie/karze*</note> *1st conjugation, Passive-Anomalous.*

**544** **The Rod**, <note>original: *Ferula* — literally the giant fennel plant, whose stalks were used for striking students' hands.</note> a switch. <note>German: *die Ruthe*; Polish: *rozga*</note> *Feminine, 1st declension.*
**At hand**, <note>original: *Præsto*</note> ready or available. *Defective noun (used as an adverbial phrase).*

**545** **I beat**, <note>original: *Verbero*</note> to strike or whip. <note>German: *schlagen*; Polish: *biie*</note> *1st conjugation, Active.*
**The Staff**, <note>original: *Baculus*</note> a stick or cane. <note>German: *der Stecke*; Polish: *laska*</note> *Masculine, 2nd declension.*
**The Club**, <note>original: *Fustis*</note> a cudgel or heavy stick. <note>German: *der Knüttel*; Polish: *piesć* (fist/hand-staff)</note> *Masculine, 3rd declension.*

**546** **I strike**, <note>original: *Cado* (likely a misspelling of *Caedo*)</note> to cut or lash. <note>German: *streichen*; Polish: *sieke*</note> *3rd conjugation, Active.*
**The Rod**, <note>original: *Virga*</note> a switch or twig. <note>German: *die Ruthe*; Polish: *rozga*</note> *Feminine, 1st declension.*

**547** **Without**, <note>original: *Absque*</note> apart from. <note>German: *ohn*; Polish: *okrom*</note> *Preposition governing the ablative case.*
**Permission**, <note>original: *Venia*</note> leave or allowance. <note>German: *die Erloubnuß*; Polish: *dozwolenie*</note> *Feminine, 1st declension.*
**I go out**, <note>original: *Exeo*</note> to exit or go forth. <note>German: *hinauß gehen*; Polish: *wychodze*</note> *4th conjugation, Neuter.*

**548** **A delay**, <note>original: *Mora*</note> a little while or a pause. <note>German: *die Weile*; Polish: *chwilka*</note> *Feminine, 1st declension.*
**I return**, <note>original: *Redeo*</note> to come back. <note>German: *wiederkommen*; Polish: *wracam sie*</note> *4th conjugation, Neuter.*

**549** **I do**, <note>original: *Ago*</note> to act or perform. <note>German: *thun*; Polish: *czynie*</note> *3rd conjugation, Active.*
**I ought**, <note>original: *Debeo*</note> should or to be obliged. <note>German: *sollen*; Polish: *powinienem*</note> *2nd conjugation, Active.*

**550** **The Line**, <note>original: *Linea*</note> a mark or stroke. <note>German: *die Linie*; Polish: *linia*</note> *Feminine, 1st declension.*
**The Ruler**, <note>original: *Regula*</note> a straightedge or guide. <note>German: *das Linial*; Polish: *prawidło*</note> *Feminine, 1st declension.*

**551** **Circle** <note>original: *Circulus*</note>, the circle / or ring <note>Polish: *okrąg*</note>. *Masculine noun, 2nd declension.*

<meta>This page continues the trilingual (Latin-German-Polish) vocabulary list used for instruction in the liberal arts. Following the previous page's introduction of the Trivium and Quadrivium figures—such as the Grammarian and the Historian—this page moves into the remaining higher sciences (Astronomy, Physics, Ethics, and Medicine) and the basic vocabulary of study and household actions. The catchword "Quum" at the top likely refers to a missing or preceding transition in the broader text, though the vocabulary sequence continues numerically.</meta>

**560** **Astronomer**, noun, masculine 2nd declension. <note>original: "Astronomus, i, der Sternseher / gwiazdarz"</note> The stargazer or practitioner of astronomy.

**I describe**, I wrote, written, to describe, 3rd conjugation active. <note>original: "Describo, scripsi, scriptum, bere, beschreiben / opisuię"</note> To write down or map out.

**Star**, noun, neuter 2nd declension. <note>original: "Astrum, i, das Gestirn / gwiazda"</note> A celestial body or constellation.

**561** **Physicist** (Natural Philosopher), noun, masculine 2nd declension. <note>original: "Physicus, i, der Naturkündiger / przyrodzonych rzeczy mistrz"</note> A master of natural things or one who studies the laws of nature.

**Nature**, noun, feminine 1st declension. <note>original: "Natúra, æ, die Natur / przyrodzenie własność"</note> The inherent properties or essence of things.

**I examine**, examined, to examine, 1st conjugation deponent. <note>original: "Scrutor, átus sum, ári, ergründen / erforschen / wywiaduię się"</note> To search out, explore, or get to the bottom of a matter.

**562** **Doctor** (Physician), noun, masculine 2nd declension. <note>original: "Medicus, i, der Arzt / lekarz"</note> A healer or medical practitioner.

**I imitate**, imitated, to imitate, 1st conjugation deponent. <note>original: "Imitor, átus sum, ári, nachthun / folgen / ahnen násladuię"</note> To follow, mimic, or copy an example.

**563** **Ethicist** (Moral Philosopher), noun, masculine 2nd declension. <note>original: "Ethicus, i, der Sittenmeister / mistrz dobrych rzeczy"</note> A master of morals or teacher of good conduct.

**I hand over**, I gave over, given over, to hand over, 3rd conjugation active. <note>original: "Trado, didi, ditum, ere, übergeben / uczę / posdaię"</note> To deliver, teach, or pass down knowledge.

**Custom**, noun, masculine 3rd declension. <note>original: "Mos, óris, die Sitte / obyczay"</note> Manner, habit, or moral practice.

**564** **Student**, noun, masculine 2nd declension. <note>original: "Studiósus, i, der Student / student / nauk pilny"</note> One who is diligent in learning or eager for knowledge.

**I adorn**, I adorned, adorned, to adorn, 1st conjugation active. <note>original: "Decoro, ávi, átum, áre, zieren / zdobię"</note> To decorate or honor.

**565** **But**, adversative conjunction. <note>original: "Verùm, aber / ále / lecz"</note> A word used to express contrast.

**A little**, adverb of degree. <note>original: "Paulò, ein wenig / trochę"</note> Slightly or by a small amount.

**Below**, adverb of place. <note>original: "Infrà, drunten / niżey"</note> Further down or underneath.

---

**566** **I knock**, I knocked, knocked, to knock, 1st conjugation active. <note>original: "Pulso, ávi, átum, áre, anklopffen / kołacę / biię"</note> To strike or beat upon (usually a door).

**Door**, noun, feminine 3rd declension. <note>original: "Foris, is, die Thür / drzwi"</note> An entrance or gate.

**I close**, I closed, [closed], to close, 3rd conjugation active. <note>original: "Claudo, clausi, sum, ere, zuschliessen / zámykam"</note> To shut or lock.

573 **Paneled ceiling** <note>original: *laquear*, German: *die Decke*, Polish: *strop*</note>, a neuter noun of the 3rd declension. <note>Specifically refers to a decorative, recessed ceiling panel.</note>
**It hangs over** or **threatens** <note>original: *impendeo*, German: *hänget über*, Polish: *wisi*</note>, a neuter verb of the 2nd conjugation.

574 **Window** <note>original: *fenestra*, German: *das Fenster*, Polish: *okno*</note>, a feminine noun of the 1st declension.
**Glass** or **made of glass** <note>original: *vitreus*, German: *gläsern*, Polish: *szklane*</note>, an adjective derived from the noun for glass.

575 **Heated room** <note>original: *hypocaustum* (Latin), *die Stube* (German), *izba* (Polish)</note>, a neuter noun of the 2nd declension. <term>Hypocaustum: specifically a room heated from below, a common luxury in Roman and later European homes to combat the winter cold.</term>

<page-num>590</page-num> **The Hand** <note>original Latin: *Manus*; German: *die Hand*; Polish: *ręká*. The numbers following the Polish words, like *F. 4.*, indicate the feminine gender and fourth declension in Polish grammar.</note>

<meta>This page continues the catalog of personal life and household management. **598** **Shirt**, <note>original Latin: *Indusium, i*</note>, the shirt <note>original German: *das Hembde* / original Polish: *koßula*</note> **Neuter, 2nd declension.**
**Tunic**, <note>original Latin: *Tunica, æ*</note>, the coat or gown <note>original German: *der Rock* / original Polish: *suknia*</note> **Feminine, 1st declension.**

**599** **Doublet**, <note>original Latin: *Thorax, ácis*</note>, the doublet or vest <note>original German: *das Wames* / original Polish: *kabat / kaftan*</note> **Masculine, 3rd declension.**
**Breeches**, <note>original Latin: *Femorále, is*</note>, the trousers or hose <note>original German: *die Hose* / original Polish: *nogawica*</note> **Neuter, 3rd declension.**
**Breeches**, <note>original Latin: *Bracca, æ*</note>, the hose, shaggy garment, or fur <note>original German: *die Hose* / original Polish: *szata kosmata / buba*</note> **Feminine, 1st declension.**
**Stocking**, <note>original Latin: *Tibiále, is*</note>, the stocking <note>original German: *der Strumpff* / original Polish: *pończochy*</note> **Neuter, 3rd declension.**

**600** **To put on**, <note>original Latin: *Induo, ui, útum, ere*</note>, to dress or put on <note>original German: *anziehen* / original Polish: *obłocze*</note> **3rd Conjugation, Active.**
**To take off**, <note>original Latin: *Exuo, ui, útum, ere*</note>, to undress or pull off <note>original German: *außziehen* / original Polish: *zwłocze / z zuwam*</note> **3rd Conjugation, Active.**

**601** **Brush**, <note>original Latin: *Scopula, æ*</note>, the whisk or clothes-brush <note>original German: *die Kehrbürste* / original Polish: *miotełka*</note> **Feminine, 1st declension.**
**To brush off**, <note>original Latin: *Everro, verri, versum, rere*</note>, to sweep out or brush clean <note>original German: *außkehren* / original Polish: *wymiatam*</note> **3rd Conjugation, Active.**

**602** **Need**, <note>original Latin: *Opus*</note>, necessary or required <note>original German: *von nöthen* / original Polish: *potrzéba jest*</note> **Defective noun, diptote.**

**603** **Cloak**, <note>original Latin: *Pallium, i*</note>, the mantle or cloak <note>original German: *der Mantel* / original Polish: *płaszcz*</note> **Neuter, 2nd declension.**
**Gown**, <note>original Latin: *Toga, æ*</note>, the long coat or robe <note>original German: *der lange Rock* / original Polish: *szata długa*</note> **Feminine, 1st declension.**
**To clothe**, <note>original Latin: *Amicio, ivi & xi, ctum, ire*</note>, to put on or dress oneself <note>original German: *anlegen* / original Polish: *ubieram / odziewam*</note> **4th Conjugation, Active.**

**604** **Hat**, <note>original Latin: *Pileus, i*</note>, the hat or cap <note>original German: *der Hutt* / original Polish: *czapka*</note> **Masculine, 2nd declension.**
**Collar**, <note>original Latin: *Colláre, is*</note>, the collar or neck-piece <note>original German: *der Krage / das Hälschen* / original Polish: *kołnierz*</note> **Neuter, 3rd declension.**

**605** **Glove**, <note>original Latin: *Chirothéca, æ*</note>, the glove <note>original German: *der Handschuh* / original Polish: *rękawica*</note> **Feminine, 1st declension.**
**Slipper**, <note>original Latin: *Sandalium, i*</note>, the slipper, patten, or old-fashioned footwear <note>original German: *der Pantoffel* / original Polish: *pátinka / obow staroświecki*</note> **Neuter, 2nd declension.**

**606** **Decency**, <note>original Latin: *Honéstas, átis*</note>, respectability or virtue <note>original German: *die Erbarkeit* / original Polish: *uczciwość*</note> **Feminine, 3rd declension.**
**Therefore**, <note>original Latin: *Ergò*</note>, because of or consequently <note>original German: *wegen / też / wiec*</note> **Defective, monoptote.**

**607** **Belt**, <note>original Latin: *Cingulum, i*</note>, the belt or girdle <note>original German: *der Gürtel* / original Polish: *pas*</note> **Neuter, 2nd declension.**
**To gird**, <note>original Latin: *Cingo, cinxi, cinctum, gere*</note>, to buckle or gird <note>original German: *gürten* / original Polish: *opásuie*</note> **3rd Conjugation, Active.**

**608** **Lace**, <note>original Latin: *Ligula, æ*</note>, the point or lace <note>original German: *der Nestel* / original Polish: *wstęga*. A "point" was a lace with a metal tip used to fasten various parts of early modern clothing.</note> **Feminine, 1st declension.**
**To tighten**, <note>original Latin: *Astringo, nxi, ictum, gere*</note>, to draw together or tie <note>original German: *zusammen ziehen* / original Polish: *przywiezuie / zadziergam*</note> **3rd Conjugation, Active.**

609 The **tablecloth**: <note>Latin: *mappa*; German: *das Tischtuch*; Polish: *obrus*. Feminine noun, 1st declension.</note>

I **spread** or **cover**: <note>Latin: *sterno*; German: *decken*; Polish: *nakrywam*. 3rd conjugation active verb.</note>
The **table**: <note>Latin: *mensa*; German: *der Tisch*; Polish: *stół*. Feminine noun, 1st declension.</note>

610 The **soup**: <note>Latin: *jusculum*; German: *die Suppe*; Polish: *polewka*. Neuter noun, 2nd declension.</note>
The **porridge**, **mash**, or **stew**: <note>Latin: *pulmentum*; German: *der Brey*; Polish: *kassa* or *potrawk*. Neuter noun, 2nd declension.</note>

The **broth** or **gravy**: <note>Latin: *jus*; German: *die Suppe*; Polish: *polewka*. Neuter noun, 3rd declension.</note>

The **hornet**: <note>Latin: *crabro*; German: *die Hörnise*; Polish: *sierszeń*. Masculine noun, 3rd declension.</note>

### Vocabulary Guide
**Tablecloth** <note>Latin: *mappa*; German: *Tischtuch*; Polish: *obrus*</note>
**Carpet or Tapestry** <note>Latin: *tapete*; German: *Teppich*; Polish: *kobierzec*</note>
**Table** <note>Latin: *mensa*; German: *Tisch*; Polish: *stół*</note>
**Soup and Porridge** <note>Latin: *jusculum, pulmentum*; German: *Suppe, Brey*; Polish: *polewka, kasza*</note>
**Spoon and Knife** <note>Latin: *cochlear, culter*; German: *Löffel, Messer*; Polish: *łyżka, nóż*</note>
**Insects (Flies, Wasps, Hornets)** <note>Latin: *musca, vespa, crabro*; German: *Fliegen, Wespen, Hörnisen*; Polish: *muchy, osy, szerszenie*</note>
**Fly-whisk** <note>Latin: *muscarium*; German: *Fliegwedel*; Polish: *oganka*</note>
**Tableware (Bowl, Plate, Salt-cellar)** <note>Latin: *patina, orbis, salinum*; German: *Schüssel, Teller, Salßfaß*; Polish: *misa, talerz, solnica*</note>
**Crumbs** <note>Latin: *mica*; German: *Brosamen*; Polish: *odrobiny*</note>

It also highlights the necessity of using a fly-whisk to protect food from common household pests like wasps and hornets.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the instructional vocabulary list related to the dining table and domestic life. Following the previous page's focus on main courses and utensils, we move to the final stages of the meal—desserts and drinking rituals—and the transition to evening lighting and general household equipment. 614 **Finally**, at last <note>original Latin: *Tandem*; German: *endlich*; Polish: *náostátek*. A temporal adverb marking the end of the meal.</note>
**Dessert**, confections <note>original Latin: *Bellaria*; German: *das Confect*. This term refers to the sweet meats, nuts, or fruits served at the end of a banquet.</note>
615 **To pour in**, or to fill [a vessel] <note>original Latin: *Infundo*; German: *einschencken*; Polish: *nálewam*.</note>
**Goblet**, beaker, or cup <note>original Latin: *Poculum*; German: *der Becher*; Polish: *kubek*.</note>
616 **To toast**, or to drink to someone <note>original Latin: *Propíno*; German: *zutrincken*; Polish: *piie do kogo*. This refers to the social ritual of pledging a drink to a guest.</note>
**Host**, and also the **Guest** <note>original Latin: *Hospes*; German: *Wirth* or *Gast*; Polish: *gość*. In Latin, the word *hospes* is reciprocal, meaning both the one who provides hospitality and the one who receives it.</note>
617 **To light**, or to kindle <note>original Latin: *Accendo*; German: *anzünden*; Polish: *zápalam*.</note>
**Wax candle**, or taper <note>original Latin: *Cereus*; German: *die Wachskerze*; Polish: *woskowa*.</note>
618 **Candle**, or light <note>original Latin: *Candéla*; German: *das Liecht*; Polish: *świecá*.</note>
**Tallow**, or suet <note>original Latin: *Sebum*; German: *das Inschlet*; Polish: *łoj*. Tallow was the rendered animal fat used to make cheaper candles, as opposed to the more expensive beeswax.</note>
**To make**, or to do <note>original Latin: *Facio*; Polish: *czynie*.</note>
619 **To place in**, or to set up <note>original Latin: *Indo*; German: *auffstecken*; Polish: *wtykam*. Here referring to placing a candle into a holder.</note>
**Candlestick**, or lampstand <note>original Latin: *Candelábrū*; German: *Leuchter*; Polish: *lichtarz*.</note>
620 **To trim** a candle, or to blow one's nose <note>original Latin: *Emungo*; German: *pußen* or *schneußen*; Polish: *ucieram świece* or *nos*. In this period, the same verb was used for "cleaning" a wick and "cleaning" the nostrils.</note>
**Wick** <note>original Latin: *Ellychnium*; German: *das Dacht* (modern *Docht*); Polish: *knot*.</note>
**Snuffers**, or candle-trimmers <note>original Latin: *Emunctorium*; German: *die Liechtpuße*; Polish: *nożyczki do świec*. Scissor-like tools used to trim the burnt wick of a candle to prevent smoking.</note>
621 **To extinguish**, or to put out <note>original Latin: *Extingvo*; German: *außleschen*; Polish: *gáśe*.</note>
622 **Lantern** <note>original Latin: *Laterna*; German: *die Latern*; Polish: *láternia*.</note>
**Tinder**, or touchwood <note>original Latin: *Fomes*; German: *der Zunder*; Polish: *żagiew*. Material used to catch a spark from flint and steel.</note>
**Sieve**, or sifter <note>original Latin: *Cribrum*; German: *das Sieb*; Polish: *sito*.</note>
**Strainer**, or colander <note>original Latin: *Colum*; German: *durchschlag*; Polish: *durszlat*.</note>
**Whetstone**, or hone <note>original Latin: *Cos*; German: *Weßstein* (modern *Wetzstein*); Polish: *ostrá*. Used for sharpening knives and tools.</note>
**Scale**, or balance; also a **pound** [weight] <note>original Latin: *Libra*; German: *die Wage*; Polish: *wagá/funt*.</note>
**Mousetrap** <note>original Latin: *Decipula*; German: *die maußfall*; Polish: *łapicá*.</note>
**Household furniture**, or domestic equipment <note>original Latin: *Supellex*; German: *der Haußrath*; Polish: *sprzęt*. A general term for all movable goods in a house.</note>
**Necessary**, or essential <note>original Latin: *Necessarius*; German: *nothwendige*; Polish: *potrzebny*.</note>

<meta>This page continues the transition from household management to the human roles within the family and the life cycle. The previous page concluded a list of domestic tools (lanterns, scales, mousetraps); this page begins a thematic dictionary of human relationships, marriage, and childbirth.</meta>

**623** **Man**, the man / the husband <note>original: *Vir* (Latin), *der Mann* (German), *mąż* (Polish)</note> *Masculine 2nd declension.*
**Woman**, the woman / the lady *Feminine 3rd declension.*
**Love**, the love / the affection *Masculine 3rd declension.*
**Join**, to join together / I unite / I harness *3rd conjugation, Active.*
**Spouse**, a husband or a wife / the spouse / the wife *Common gender 3rd declension.* <note>The "C" indicates common gender, meaning the noun can be used for either a male or female partner.</note>

**624** **Widower**, the widower / the widower *Masculine 2nd declension.*
**Lead**, to take [a wife] / I lead / I take *3rd conjugation, Active.* <note>In Latin, the idiom for a man marrying is "to lead a wife" (*ducere uxorem*).</note>
**Sometimes**, occasionally / sometimes *adverb of time.*
**Virgin**, the young woman / the maiden *Feminine 3rd declension.*

**625** **Bridegroom**, the bridegroom / the groom / the newlywed *Masculine 2nd declension.*
**Marry**, to take a husband / he marries <note>original: *żeni się*</note> *3rd conjugation, Active.* <note>Strictly speaking, the Latin *nubo* refers to the woman "veiling herself" for a husband.</note>
**Bride**, the bride / the bride *Feminine 1st declension.*

**626** **Wedding**, the wedding / the feast / the celebration *Feminine 1st declension, plural only.*
**Husband**, the husband / the married man / the husband *Masculine 2nd declension.*
**Wife**, the wife / the married woman / the wife *Feminine 1st declension.*
**Call**, to name / I call / I name *1st conjugation, Active.*

**627** **New Mother**, the woman in labor / the woman in her six-weeks of recovery / the woman giving birth *Feminine 1st declension.* <note>Refers to *puerperium*, the traditional period of rest for a mother after childbirth.</note>
**Bring forth**, to give birth / I bear *3rd conjugation, Active.*

<meta>This page continues the catalog of household members and family relations.
