Minister, istri: The servant or manager. <note>original Latin: *Minister*; German: *der diener*; Polish: *sprawca*. The Latin term implies someone who carries out a task for another, while the Polish *sprawca* here suggests an administrator or agent.</note> <note>Masculine, 2nd declension</note>
Ministro, avi, atum, are: To serve or assist. <note>original Latin: *Ministro*; German: *dienen*; Polish: *służę/posługuję*</note> <note>1st conjugation, Neuter/Intransitive</note>

Famulus, i: The servant or house-hand. <note>original Latin: *Famulus*; German: *der Diener*; Polish: *czeladnik* (apprentice/household member) or *sługa* (servant).</note> <note>Masculine, 2nd declension</note>
Famulor, atus sum, ari: To serve. <note>original Latin: *Famulor*; German: *dienen*; Polish: *służę*</note> <note>1st conjugation, Deponent</note>

642 Ancilla, ae: The maidservant or young girl. <note>original Latin: *Ancilla*; German: *die Magd*; Polish: *dziewka*</note> <note>Feminine, 1st declension</note>
Ancillor, atus sum, ari: To perform the services of a maidservant. <note>original Latin: *Ancillor*; German: *Mågde Dienste leisten*; Polish: *służę/przysługuję*</note> <note>1st conjugation, Deponent</note>

643 Patruus, i: The paternal uncle. <note>original Latin: *Patruus*; German: *der Vetter*; Polish: *stryj*. Note: Early Modern German and Polish were more specific than modern English; *stryj* refers specifically to a father's brother.</note> <note>Masculine, 2nd declension</note>
Amita, ae: The paternal aunt. <note>original Latin: *Amita*; German: *die Base*; Polish: *stryjenka* (uncle's wife) or *ciotka po ojcu* (aunt on the father's side).</note> <note>Feminine, 1st declension</note>

644 Pater, tris: The father. <note>original Latin: *Pater*; German: *der Vater*; Polish: *ociec*</note> <note>Masculine, 3rd declension</note>
Frater, tris: The brother. <note>original Latin: *Frater*; German: *der Bruder*; Polish: *brat*</note> <note>Masculine, 3rd declension</note>
Soror, oris: The sister. <note>original Latin: *Soror*; German: *die Schwester*; Polish: *siostra*</note> <note>Feminine, 3rd declension</note>

645 Avunculus, i: The maternal uncle. <note>original Latin: *Avunculus*; German: *der Oheim*; Polish: *wuj*. Unlike the paternal uncle, the *wuj* is specifically the mother's brother.</note> <note>Masculine, 2nd declension</note>
Matertera, ae: The maternal aunt. <note>original Latin: *Matertera*; German: *die Muhm*; Polish: *ciotka po matce* (aunt on the mother's side).</note> <note>Feminine, 1st declension</note>
Mater, tris: The mother. <note>original Latin: *Mater*; German: *die Mutter*; Polish: *matka*</note> <note>Feminine, 3rd declension</note>

646 Avus, i: The grandfather. <note>original Latin: *Avus*; German: *der Großvater*; Polish: *dziad*</note> <note>Masculine, 2nd declension</note>
Habeo, ui, itum, ere: To have. <note>original Latin: *Habeo*; German: *haben*; Polish: *mam*</note> <note>2nd conjugation, Active</note>
Nepos, otis: The grandson. <note>original Latin: *Nepos*; German: *der Enckel*; Polish: *wnuk*</note> <note>Masculine, 3rd declension</note>

647 Cognatus, i: The relative or kinsman. <note>original Latin: *Cognatus*; German: *der Verwandte*; Polish: *pokrewny/powinny*. Refers specifically to blood relations.</note> <note>Masculine, 2nd declension</note>
Affinis, is: The relative by marriage. <note>original Latin: *Affinis*; German: *der Schwager* (brother-in-law); Polish: *powinowaty z małżeństwa*. This term covers what we call "in-laws."</note> <note>Common gender, 3rd declension</note>

<meta>This page continues from the transition at the end of Chapter V, moving from the private household and family relations into the public sphere. 648 **City**, the city <note>original: *Urbs*</note> / a town.

649 **Rampart**, the wall <note>original: *Vallum*</note> / an embankment.

650 **Gate**, the gate <note>original: *Porta*</note> / an entrance.
**Folding doors**, the leaves of a gate <note>original: *Valvæ*. These refer to double doors that swing open.</note>.

651 **From here**, from that place <note>original: *Abhinc*</note> / hence.
**To go**, to walk <note>original: *Eo*</note> / I go.

652 **Marketplace**, the market / the town square <note>original: *Forum*. Historically the center of public and commercial life.</note> / a ring.
**To be accustomed**, to be in the habit of / as is wont.
**Cistern**, the cistern / a water-catcher / a reservoir or well-casing.

653 **Building**, the structure <note>original: *Ædificium*</note> / an edifice.
**Public**, that which is common <note>original: *Publicus*</note> / belonging to the community or general public.
**Private**, that which is separate <note>original: *Privátus*</note> / individual or personal.

654 **One**, a single one <note>original: *Unus*</note> / one person or thing.
**Owner**, the possessor <note>original: *Possessor*</note> / a landlord or holder of property.

**Common**, that which is shared <note>original: *Commúnis*</note> / joint or mutual.

655 **Citizen**, the burgher <note>original: *Civis*</note> / a city inhabitant.
**Inhabitant**, the resident <note>original: *Incola*</note> / a dweller.
**To inhabit**, to dwell / to live in a certain place.
**City-State**, the city <note>original: *Civitas*. This can refer to the physical city or the body of citizens making up the state.</note>.

| 652 In the marketplace, there are usually cisterns: <note>original: "In foro solent esse cisternæ."</note> | On the market (the square), there are usually cisterns (water-catchers) or pipe-boxes: <note>original: "Auff dem Marckt (Ring) pflegen zu seyn Cistern (Wasserfänge) od Röhrkasten"</note> | In the market, there are usually wells: <note>original: "Ná rynku zwykły bywać krynice"</note> |
| 653 Buildings are public, or private. <note>original: "Ædificia sunt publica, aut priváta."</note> | The buildings are common or separate. <note>original: "Die Gebäwe sind gemein / oder sondere."</note> | Buildings are general or individual. <note>original: "Budowánia są ogolne abo osobne."</note> |
| 654 For what is not the property of one owner, is common. <note>original: "Nam quod nō uni possessóri proprium est, est comūne"</note> | For what is not owned by one possessor, that is common. <note>original: "Deñ was nicht einem Besitzer eigen ist / das ist gemein."</note> | For (because) what is not the property of one landlord, that is shared. <note>original: "Abowiem (bo) co nie jest własne iednego dziedzicá / to jest spolne."</note> |
| 655 Citizens and inhabitants dwell in the city. <note>original: "Cives & incolæ incolunt civitátem."</note> | The citizens and inhabitants dwell in the city. <note>original: "Die Bürger und Einwohner bewohnen die Stadt."</note> | The townspeople and inhabitants live in the city. <note>original: "Mieszczánie y obywatele mieszkają w mieście."</note> |

<meta>This page continues the legal and social progression from the previous page. **666** **To lead** <term>Duco</term> (to guide or conduct) <note>original: "führen / wiode / prowádzę"</note> *3rd conjugation, active verb.*
**The prison** <term>Carcer</term> (the jail or cell) <note>original: "das Gefängnuß / więzienie"</note> *Masculine, 3rd declension.*

**667** **The executioner** <term>Carnifex</term> (the hangman or headsman) <note>original: "der Hencker / kát"</note> *Masculine, 3rd declension.*
**To kill** <term>Occido</term> (to slay or put to death) <note>original: "tödten / zabiiam"</note> *3rd conjugation, active verb.*
**To behead** <term>Decollo</term> (to decapitate) <note>original: "köpffen / ścinam"</note> *1st conjugation, active verb.*
**To hang** <term>Suspendo</term> (to execute by the neck) <note>original: "hencken / wießam"</note> *3rd conjugation, active verb.*

**668** **The messenger** <term>Tabellarius</term> (a courier or bearer of tablets) <note>original: "der Bothe / poseł"</note> *Masculine, 2nd declension.*
**Letters** <term>Literæ</term> (the written message) <note>original: "der Brieff / listy"</note> *Feminine, 1st declension, used only in the plural.*
**The letter** <term>Epistola</term> (the epistle) <note>original: "der Brieff / list"</note> *Feminine, 1st declension.*

**669** **The wax** <term>Cera</term> (used for sealing letters) <note>original: "das Wachs / wosk"</note> *Feminine, 1st declension.*
**The seal** <term>Sigillum</term> (the signet or impression) <note>original: "das Siegel / Petschafft / pieczęć"</note> *Neuter, 2nd declension.*
**To seal up** <term>Obsigno</term> (to close with a seal) <note>original: "versiegeln / verpetschiesren / pieczętuię"</note> *1st conjugation, active verb.*

**670** **The herald** <term>Nuncius</term> (the oral messenger or announcer) <note>original: "der Mundbothe / posłániec / opowiádacz"</note> *Masculine, 2nd declension.*
**To announce** <term>Nuncio</term> (to declare or make known) <note>original: "verkündigen / oznáymuię"</note> *1st conjugation, active verb.*
**The mouth** <term>Os</term> <note>original: "der Mund / usta"</note> *Neuter, 3rd declension.*
**As far as** <term>Tenus</term> (up to or reaching to) <note>original: "biß an / potyl"</note> *Preposition governing the ablative case.*

**671** **The church** <term>Templum</term> (the temple or sacred building) <note>original: "die Kirch / kościoł"</note> *Neuter, 2nd declension.*
**Holy** <term>Sacer</term> (sacred or consecrated) <note>original: "heilige / świety"</note> *Adjective of three terminations.*
**The place** <term>Locus</term> (the spot or location) <note>original: "der Ort / mieysce"</note> *Masculine, 2nd declension.*

**672** **The tavern** <term>Caupóna</term> (the inn or drinking-house) <note>original: "die Schencke / kárczmá"</note> *Feminine, 1st declension.*
**Profane** <term>Profánus</term> (unholy, secular, or common) <note>original: "unheilige / niepoświecony"</note> *Adjective.*
**The cook-shop** <term>Popína</term> (a public kitchen or victualling-house) <note>original: "die Garküche / kuchnia mieyska"</note> *Feminine, 1st declension.*
**The inn** <term>Diversorium</term> (a lodging-place for travelers) <note>original: "die Herberge / gospodá"</note> *Neuter, 2nd declension.*

**673** **The sacristan** <term>Ædituus</term> (the sexton, churchwarden, or bell-ringer) <note>original: "der Glöckner / Küster / kościelny / dzwonnik"</note> *Masculine, 2nd declension.*
**To ring** <term>Pulso</term> (to strike or beat the bell) <note>original: "lauten / dzwonię / kołácę"</note> *1st conjugation, active verb.*
**The bell** <term>Campána</term> <note>original: "die Glocke / dzwon"</note> *Feminine, 1st declension.*

<meta>This page continues the thematic organization of society, transitioning from the physical structures of the church and public life mentioned on page 673 to the spiritual and political order of the community. It lists vocabulary related to religious practice, the liturgy, and the hierarchy of the state.</meta>

674 **The People** <term>Populus</term> <note>original: "Populus, i, das Volck / lud M. 2." — Masculine, 2nd declension.</note>
**To assemble** <term>Coëo</term> (to come together) <note>original: "Coëo, ivi, itum, ire, zuſamen komen / schadzaią ſie"</note>
**The Church or Temple** <term>Ædes</term> <note>original: "Ædes, is. die Kirche / kośćioł F. 3. (4. N." — The Latin term can refer to a house or a sanctuary; here specifically the religious building.</note>

675 **To practice or exercise** <term>Exerceo</term> <note>original: "Exerceo, ui, itum, ére, üben / ćwicze / ucze 2. A." — 2nd conjugation, active. The Polish implies both practicing and teaching.</note>
**Religion** <term>Religio</term> <note>original: "Religio, ónis, die Religion / nabożeńſtwo F. 3." — The Polish term refers to piety or a religious service.</note>

676 **The Assembly or Congregation** <term>Cœtus</term> <note>original: "Cœtus, ûs, die verſamlung / zgromadźenie M. 4." — Masculine, 4th declension.</note>
**To sing** <term>Cano</term> <note>original: "Cano, cecini, cantum, ere, ſingen / śpiewam 3. A." — 3rd conjugation, active.</note>
**The Psalm** <term>Pſalmus</term> <note>original: "Pſalmus, i, der Pſalm / Pſalm M. 2."</note>
**The Hymn** <term>Hymnus</term> <note>original: "Hymnus, i, der Lobgeſang / hymn M. 2." — A song of praise.</note>

677 **God** <term>Deus</term> <note>original: "Deus, i, der Gott / Bog M. 2."</note>
**The Word** <term>Verbum</term> <note>original: "Verbum, i, das Wort / słowo N. 2." — Neuter, 2nd declension.</note>
**To preach or proclaim** <term>Prædico</term> <note>original: "Prædico, ávi, átū, áre, predigē / przepowiadam 1 A." — 1st conjugation, active.</note>

678 **The Sacrament** <term>Sacramentum</term> <note>original: "Sacramentū, i, das Sacrament / ſácráment N. 2"</note>
**To administer or serve** <term>Adminiſtro</term> <note>original: "Adminiſtro, ávi, átum, áre, darreichen / poſłu:guie / rządzę 1. A." — Literally "to reach out" or "to manage."</note>

679 **Prayer** <term>Preces</term> <note>original: "Precis, das Gebet / modlitwá F. 3. Def." — Defective noun, usually appearing in the plural.</note>
**To perform or complete** <term>Perago</term> <note>original: "Perago, égi, actū, gere, verzichten / odprawuie 3. A." — Note: The German 'verzichten' (to waive) is likely a transcription or period error for 'verrichten' (to perform), matching the Latin and Polish.</note>
**Devoutly** <term>Devótè</term> <note>original: "Devótè, andächtig / nabożnie adv. qual." — An adverb of quality.</note>

680 **The Feast Day or Holiday** <term>Feſtum</term> <note>original: "Feſtum, i, der Feſttag / święto N. 2."</note>
**To celebrate or observe** <term>Celebro</term> <note>original: "Celebro, ávi, átū, áre, begehen / świecę / chwalę 1 A." — The Polish terms include "to consecrate" and "to praise."</note>
**Festively or solemnly** <term>Feſtivè</term> <note>original: "Feſtivè, feyerlich / wdzięcznie / dobrze adv. qual."</note>

681 **The Heathen or Pagan** <term>Ethnicus</term> <note>original: "Ethnicus, i, der Heide / pogánie M. 2."</note>
**The Church or Christian Community** <term>Eccleſia</term> <note>original: "Eccleſia, æ, die Chriſtliche Gemeine / kośćioł / zgromadźenie / zbor F. 1." — Refers to the collective body of believers.</note>

682 **The Magistrate or Authority** <term>Magiſtrátus</term> <note>original: "Magiſtrátus, ûs, die Obrigkeit / zwierzchność M. 4"</note>
**Necessary** <term>Neceſſarius</term> <note>original: "Neceſſarius, a, um, der die das nothwendige / potrzebny / a / e / pożyteczny / a / e, adj. 3." — An adjective of three terminations.</note>

<meta>This page continues the thematic organization of the social and political hierarchy. 684 **Lord or Master** <note>original: Latin *Dominus*; German *der Herr*; Polish *pan*</note> *Masculine noun, 2nd declension.*

685 **To command or bid** <note>original: Latin *Impero*; German *gebieten*; Polish *rozkazuie*</note> *1st conjugation, active verb.*

686 **To obey or be compliant** <note>original: Latin *Pareo*; German *gehorsam seyn*; Polish *dogadzam* (I satisfy/cater to)</note> *2nd conjugation, neuter verb.*

**Tribute, Rent, or Income** <note>original: Latin *Census*; German *der Zinß*; Polish *dochod* (income), *czynß* (rent), *pobor* (levy)</note> *Masculine noun, 4th declension.*
**Ready or Willing** <note>original: Latin *Promptus*; German *willige*; Polish *posłuszny* (obedient)</note> *Adjective, 3 endings.* <note>This indicates the word has distinct forms for masculine, feminine, and neuter genders.</note>

689 **Master of the House or Host** <note>original: Latin *Herus*; German *der Herr*; Polish *gospodarz*, *pan*</note> *Masculine noun, 2nd declension.*

### ON VIRTUES

<margin>710</margin> Pursue **virtues**.

<margin>711</margin> Shun **vices**, which the law forbids.

<margin>713</margin> Is a **wicked scoundrel**.

<margin>715</margin> Look ahead to the **end**:

### CHAPTER VII.
### Of Virtues.

Chase after the **virtues** (apply yourself to virtues).

Flee the **vices** which the law forbids.

He who commits (**perpetrates**) **misdeeds**:

Is a **shameful knave** (**villain**).

In the meantime, so that you may be **cautious** (**wise**):

Look first to the **end**:

<column-break/>

With **cannons** they conquer castles.

They return with **booty** (**spoil**) and victory.

### CHAPTER VII.
### ABOUT VIRTUES.

Follow **virtues**.

Protect yourself from **faults** which the law <note>original Polish: *zakon* — in modern Polish this means a religious order, but in this historical context, it refers to "law" or "statute"</note> forbids.

The one who has committed **disgrace** (**shame**, **insult**).

Is a **malicious rogue**.

Furthermore, so that you may be **prudent**:

Watch the **end**.

736 **Silent / Quiet** <note>original: "Taciturnus"</note>, the quiet or reserved one. *adjective.*

740 **Hidden** <note>original: "Occulta"</note>, things that are concealed or kept out of sight. *plural neuter adjective.*
741 **Unknown** <note>original: "Ignota"</note>, things that are unfamiliar or strange. *plural neuter adjective.*

| 782 In prosperity he is exalted. <note>original Latin: *In prosperis*; Polish: *W szcześciu*. This describes how a weak person becomes arrogant when things go well.</note> | In welfare (by good luck) he raises himself up. | In fortune he exalts himself (boasts/puffs up). |

786 **I try**, I attempt. <note>original Latin: *Conor*; German: *versuchen*; Polish: *usiłuię*. Grammatical code: 1st conjugation deponent verb.</note>

787 **I ought**, I must. <note>original: *Debeo*; German: *sollen*; Polish: *powinienem*. 2nd conjugation active verb.</note>
**First**, in the beginning. <note>original: *Priùs*; German: *erstlich*; Polish: *wprzód*. Temporal adverb.</note>
**I inquire**, I search out, I ask. <note>original: *Contor* (likely a variant of *percunctor*); German: *forschen/nachfragen*; Polish: *pytam/wywiáduię ſię*. 1st conjugation deponent verb.</note>

788 **I attack**, I approach, I begin. <note>original: *Aggredior*; German: *angreiffen*; Polish: *przyſtępuię/poczynam*. 3rd conjugation deponent verb.</note>

789 **More**, further. <note>original: *Ampliùs*; German: *mehr*; Polish: *wiecey*. Comparative adverb.</note>
**I delay**, I linger, I procrastinate. <note>original: *Cūnctor*; German: *sich seumen*; Polish: *odwłaczam/ociągam się*. 1st conjugation deponent verb.</note>

790 **Fortune**, luck, fate. <note>original: *Fortúna*; German: *das Glück*; Polish: *szczęście*. Feminine noun, 1st declension.</note>
**Inconstant**, unsteady, changing. <note>original: *Inconſtans*; German: *unbeständige*; Polish: *nieſtáteczny*. Adjective of one termination.</note>

791 **I bring to**, I report, I offer. <note>original: *Affero*; German: *bringen*; Polish: *przynoſzę*. 3rd conjugation active irregular verb.</note>
**Wretched**, miserable, pitiable. <note>original: *Miſer*; German: *elende*; Polish: *nędzny*. Adjective.</note>
**Chance**, accident, event, or a fall. <note>original: *Caſus*; German: *der Zufall*; Polish: *przypadek*. Masculine noun, 4th declension.</note>

792 **Patient**, enduring. <note>original: *Patiens*; German: *gedultige*; Polish: *cierpliwy*. Adjective.</note>
**I suffer**, I endure, I allow. <note>original: *Patior*; German: *leiden*; Polish: *cierpię*. 3rd conjugation deponent verb.</note>

793 **Even if**, although. <note>original: *Etiamſi*; German: *obschon*; Polish: *chociażby*. Concessive conjunction.</note>
**Although**, though. <note>original: *Etſi*; German: *obgleich*; Polish: *acz*. Concessive conjunction.</note>
**I sigh**, I groan. <note>original: *Gemo*; German: *seuffßen*; Polish: *wzdycham*. 3rd conjugation neuter (intransitive) verb.</note>
**I weep**, I cry. <note>original: *Fleo*; German: *weinen*; Polish: *płaczę*. 2nd conjugation neuter verb.</note>
**I wail**, I lament, I cry out. <note>original: *Ploro*; German: *greinen/schreien*; Polish: *krzyczę/żałuię*. 1st conjugation neuter verb.</note>

**796** **I entreat**, *having entreated, to entreat* <note>original Latin: *Deprecor, atus sum, ari*</note>. To beg off / I apologize / I beg. <note>The notations "1. D." indicate a first-conjugation deponent verb, where the form is passive but the meaning is active.</note>
**I forgive**, *I have known, the thing known, to overlook* <note>original Latin: *Ignosco, ignovi, ignotum, scere*</note>. To make allowance for / I forgive / I yield. <note>"3. A." indicates a third-conjugation active verb.</note>

**797** **I harm**, *I have harmed, harmed, to harm* <note>original Latin: *Lædo, læsi, læsum, dere*</note>. To injure / I offend. <note>3. A.</note>
**I grant**, *I have granted, granted, to grant* <note>original Latin: *Condóno, ávi, átum, áre*</note>. To gift / to remit / I forgive / I grant. <note>1. A.</note>
**Fault**, *of a fault* <note>original Latin: *Culpa, æ*</note>. The debt / guilt. <note>"F. 1." identifies this as a feminine noun of the first declension.</note>

**798** **I offend**, *I have struck, struck, to strike against* <note>original Latin: *Offendo, di, sum, dere*</note>. To insult / I offend. <note>3. A.</note>
**I pacify**, *I have pacified, pacified, to pacify* <note>original Latin: *Paco, ávi, átum, áre*</note>. To satisfy / to quiet / I reconcile. <note>1. A.</note>
**I appease**, *I have appeased, appeased, to appease* <note>original Latin: *Placo, ávi, átum, áre*</note>. To reconcile / I beg. <note>1. A.</note>

**799** **I obtain by prayer**, *I have obtained, obtained, to obtain* <note>original Latin: *Exóro, ávi, átum, áre*</note>. To request / I beseech. <note>1. A.</note>
**I cease**, *I have left off, left off, to leave off* <note>original Latin: *Desíno, desií, desitum, nere*</note>. To stop / I cease / I abandon. <note>3. act.</note>
**I am angry**, *to be angry* <note>original Latin: *Irascor, irasci*</note>. To be angry / I am angry. <note>3. D.</note>

**800** **Injury**, *of an injury* <note>original Latin: *Injuria, æ*</note>. The wrong / harm. <note>F. 1.</note>
**I endure**, *I have endured, endured, to endure* <note>original Latin: *Tolero, ávi, átum, áre*</note>. To suffer / I suffer / I endure. <note>1. A.</note>
**I avenge**, *having avenged, to avenge* <note>original Latin: *Ulciscor, ultus sum, ulcisci*</note>. To avenge / I avenge myself. <note>3 D.</note>

**801** **Malicious**, *masculine, feminine, neuter* <note>original Latin: *Malignus, a, um*</note>. He, she, or it that is spiteful / malicious. <note>"adj. 3" indicates a three-termination adjective.</note>
**I curse**, *I have spoken evil, spoken evil, to speak evil* <note>original Latin: *Maledíco, dixi, dictum, cere*</note>. To curse / I speak evil / I curse. <note>3. A.</note>

**892** <note>This is likely a printer's error for 802.</note> **Stupid**, *of a fool* <note>original Latin: *Stolidus, i*</note>. The fool / stupid / irrational. <note>"M. 2" indicates a masculine noun of the second declension.</note>

**803** **Superior**, *masculine/feminine, neuter* <note>original Latin: *Superior, us*</note>. He, she, or it that is higher / foremost / older. <note>"adj. 2. term. comp." indicates a two-termination comparative adjective.</note>

### On Kindness to Inferiors

| English (from Latin) | English (from German) | English (from Polish) |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 805 **Be affable** to your **inferior** : <note>original: *Inferióris affabilis*. The Latin uses a genitive/adjective pairing suggesting "to an inferior, be easy to speak to."</note> | **To the lower one, be talkative** : | **Gentle** to the **lower** ones. |
| 806 And you will be **lovable** : | So you will be **dear** : | You will be **dear**. |
| 807 And you will **please everyone**. | And **please all**. | And you will **be pleasing to everyone**. |
| 808 Whoever is **humane**, | Whoever is **affable** <note>original: *leutselig*. A classic German term for someone who is kind and approachable to common people.</note> / | Whoever is **human** / |
| 809 **Greets** those he meets : | He **greets** those / who **encounter** him | He **greets** those he **encounters** : |

| 814 He **comforts** (solaces) the sorrowful : | He **comforts** the saddened : | He **comforts** the sad. |
| 815 He **instructs** the **ignorant**— <note>The Latin ends mid-word with "in-", which links to the next page's expected start.</note> | He **instructs** the unknowing : | He **trains** the ignorant (unskilled) : |

**Vocabulary Study**
*   **Lower / Inferior** <note>original Latin: *Inferióris*</note>: Refers here to those of a lower social or professional rank.
*   **Affable** <note>original Latin: *affabilis*</note>: Literally "easy to speak to"; approachable.
*   **Lovable / Dear** <note>original Latin: *amabilis*</note>: Worthy of being loved or liked by others.
*   **You will please** <note>original Latin: *placébis*</note>: Future tense; a promise of social success.
*   **Humane / Affable** <note>original Latin: *humánus*; German: *leutselig*</note>: Possessing the qualities of a civilized, kind person.
*   **Greets** <note>original Latin: *salútat*; Polish: *pozdrawia*</note>: To acknowledge someone with a salute or hello.
*   **Brings help** <note>original Latin: *opiculátur*; Polish: *dopomaga*</note>: To provide aid or succor to those in distress.
*   **Comforts** <note>original Latin: *solátur*; German: *tröstet*; Polish: *cießy*</note>: To give emotional support or "solace" to the grieving.
*   **Instructs / Trains** <note>original German: *vnterweiset*; Polish: *ćwiczy*</note>: The act of teaching those who lack knowledge.

Reader, [of the] reader, the Reader / person reading. <note>original Latin: *Lector, óris*; German: *der Leser*; Polish: *czytelnik*.</note> Masculine noun, 3rd declension.
**843** Remaining (he, she, it), the remaining / left over. <note>original Latin: *Reliquus, a, um*; German: *der die das übrige*; Polish: *pozostały/a/e*.</note> Adjective of 3 endings.
I find, I found, [having been] found, to find, to discover / I encounter. <note>original Latin: *Reperio, peri, pertum, ire*; German: *finden*; Polish: *nayduie*.</note> 4th conjugation, Active.
Order, [of] order, the Order / arrangement. <note>original Latin: *Ordo, dinis*; German: *die Ordnung*; Polish: *porządek*.</note> Masculine noun, 3rd declension.

<meta>catchword: Gate (Janua)</meta>

The catchword "Ja-" at the bottom indicates the transition to the next volume, the "Janua Linguarum Reserata" (The Gate of Tongues Unlocked).</meta>

| 834 O sinner, death will devour you: <note>original Latin: *O peccátor, mors te devorábit*</note> | O you sinner / death will eat you : <note>original German: *O du Sünder / der Todt wird dich fressen*</note> | O sinner, death will consume you. <note>original Polish: *O grześniku śmierć cie pożrze*</note> |

**838** I fear, I feared, to fear / to dread. <note>original Latin: *Formido, ávi, átum, áre*; German: *erschrecke*; Polish: *lekai/boy*.</note> 1st conjugation verb.
Hell, [of] hell, the Hell / the lower world. <note>original Latin: *Infernum, i*; German: *die Helle*; Polish: *piekło*.</note> Neuter noun, 2nd declension.
**839** I desire, I desired, to desire / to long for. <note>original Latin: *Desidero, ávi, átum, áre*; German: *sehnen*; Polish: *prágnąć*.</note> 1st conjugation verb.
Heaven, [of] heaven, the Heaven / the sky. <note>original Latin: *Coelum, i*; German: *der Himmel*; Polish: *niebo*.</note> Neuter noun, 2nd declension.
**840** To sin / to do wrong. <note>original Latin: *Peccáre*; German: *sündigen*; Polish: *grzeßyć*.</note> Present infinitive.
Lest, so that... not. <note>original Latin: *Ne*; German: *daß nicht*; Polish: *ábyś nie*.</note> Negative conjunction.
I perish, I perished, to perish / to be lost. <note>original Latin: *Pereo, perii, perire*; German: *vmbkommen*; Polish: *zginąć*.</note> Irregular verb.
**841** Here / in this place. <note>original Latin: *Hic*; German: *allhie*; Polish: *tu*.</note> Adverb of place.
We stop, we remain, we stand still. <note>original Latin: *Subsistimus*; German: *stehen still*; Polish: *ustáiemy*.</note> 3rd conjugation verb, 1st person plural.
We add / we join to. <note>original Latin: *Addimus*; German: *thun hinzu*; Polish: *przydáiemy*.</note> 3rd conjugation verb, 1st person plural.
More / further. <note>original Latin: *Plus*; German: *mehr*; Polish: *wiecey*.</note> Comparative adverb.
**842** Rest, be satisfied / acquiesce. <note>original Latin: *Acquiesce*; German: *begnüge dich*; Polish: *zostań*.</note> 3rd conjugation verb, imperative.
Reader, [of the] reader, the Reader. <note>original Latin: *Lector, óris*; German: *der Leser*; Polish: *czytelnik*.</note> Masculine noun, 3rd declension.
**843** The remaining things / the rest. <note>original Latin: *Reliqua*; German: *das übrige*; Polish: *inße*.</note> Neuter plural adjective used as a noun.
You will find / you shall discover. <note>original Latin: *Reperies*; German: *wirstu finden*; Polish: *najdzieß*.</note> 4th conjugation verb, future tense.
In order / by rank / in succession. <note>original Latin: *Ordine*; German: *nach der Ordnung*; Polish: *porżadkiem*.</note> Ablative noun used adverbially.

<term>memento mori</term> — A Latin phrase meaning "Remember you must die." This was a central theme in 17th-century education and art, intended to encourage students to lead a moral life by keeping their eventual mortality in mind.

<summary>This page concludes the first book of Comenius's language system with a "memento mori" warning and an invitation for the student to proceed to the next volume. It serves as both a linguistic exercise and a moral closing to the introductory curriculum.</summary>
<keywords>sinner, death, grave, darkness, hell, heaven, sin, reader, finality, memento mori, Comenius, Vestibulum</keywords>

<meta>This page serves as the concluding vocabulary index for the "Vestibulum," the introductory portion of Comenius’s language system. It transitions the reader toward the next volume, titled "Janua" (The Gate). The entries provided here act as a summary of the religious and moral themes that closed the previous sections, focusing on piety, salvation, and eternity.</meta>

844 **Janua**, door or gate <note>original: Latin *Janua*, German *die Thür*, Polish *drzwi*</note>. *Feminine noun, 1st declension.*
**Ingredior**, to enter or go in <note>original: German *eingehen*, Polish *wchodze*</note>. *3rd conjugation, Deponent verb.*
845 **antequàm**, before or earlier <note>original: German *ehe*, Polish *niź/wprzod*</note>. *Temporal adverb.*
**Abeo**, to depart or go away <note>original: German *weggehen*, Polish *odchodze*</note>. *4th conjugation, Neuter/Intransitive verb.*
846 **Oro**, to pray or entreat <note>original: German *beten*, Polish *modle sie*</note>. *1st conjugation, Active verb.*
847 **Misereor**, to have mercy or take pity <note>original: German *sich erbarmen*, Polish *lituie sie*</note>. *2nd conjugation, Deponent verb.*
848 **Benignus**, kind, favorable, or good <note>original: German *gütige*, Polish *dobrotliwy*</note>. *Adjective of three endings.*
**Salvátor**, Savior <note>original: German *Heyland*, Polish *zbawiciel*</note>. *Masculine noun, 3rd declension.*
849 **Jesus**, Jesus. *Masculine noun, 4th declension, irregular.*
**Christus**, Christ or the Anointed One <note>original: Polish *pomazániec* means "Anointed One"</note>. *Masculine noun, 2nd declension.*
850 **Do**, to give <note>original: German *geben*, Polish *daie*</note>. *1st conjugation, Active verb.*
**Scientia**, knowledge, wisdom, or science <note>original: German *Wissenschaft*, Polish *wiadomość*</note>. *Feminine noun, 1st declension.*
851 **Pietas**, piety or godliness <note>original: German *Gottseligkeit*, Polish *pobozność*</note>. *Feminine noun, 3rd declension.*
852 **Beatitúdo**, blessedness or eternal happiness <note>original: German *Seligkeit*, Polish *błogosławieństwo*</note>. *Feminine noun, 3rd declension.*
853 **Benedictus**, blessed or praised <note>original: German *gelobte*, Polish *błogosłáwiony*</note>. *Adjective of three endings.*
**Seculum**, eternity or an age <note>original: German *Ewigkeit*, Polish *wiek*</note>. *Neuter noun, 2nd declension.*

->With the hand of God.<- <note>original Greek: *Σὺν τῇ τοῦ Θεοῦ παλάμῃ*</note>

--- Page 220 ---
<meta>This page serves as the final "Errata" or correction sheet for the *Vestibulum*, the introductory book of John Amos Comenius’s language system. Following the closing prayer on the previous page, this section ensures the student and teacher have the most accurate version of the text. It also features a library stamp from a later period, indicating this specific copy once belonged to the Imperial Library of the Pantheon in Paris.</meta>

# Let the errors be corrected thus:

Number 19: read: Augustus <note>original: *Anguſtus*</note>. Number 52: Parrot <note>original: *Pfittacus*</note> [item] 139.
Action 3, [item] 205: I grunt <note>original: *grannio*; likely a correction for the verb *grunnio*, describing the sound of a pig</note>. [item] 225: Masculine 2 <note>Grammatical note referring to the gender and declension of a noun</note>. [item] 263: Masculine 3. [item] 275: Masculine 2. [item] 273: with feet <note>original: *pedibus*</note>. [item] 295: Neuter 2. [item] 310: it is healed <note>original: *ſanatur*</note>. [item] 356: Feminine 1. [item] 470: for us <note>original: *nobis*</note>. [item] 531: Neuter 2. [item] 594: Masculine 3. [item] 745: I obey <note>original: *obtempero*</note>. [item] 763: generous <note>original: *largus*</note>. [item] 770: you have received <note>original: *accepifti*</note>.
